# Cricket Scoring Book — Implementation Task Backlog

| | |
|---|---|
| **Document** | Implementation Task Backlog |
| **Version** | 0.1.0 (Draft — for review) |
| **Date** | 2026-09-22 |
| **Upstream** | `docs/master-specification.md` v0.1.0 (exclusively, per its own precedence rule), decomposed using `docs/ai-development-harness.md`'s task schema (§4) and sizing discipline. |
| **Downstream** | The first `TASK-####` records `ai-development-harness.md`'s workflow will run — Context → Prompt → Plan → Implement → Test → Verify → Review → Update Context, per task. |
| **Status** | A **task breakdown**, not implementation. No code is written here, no repository scaffolding created, no task has been started. This document answers "what is the first buildable unit, precisely," not "here is the build." |

> **Do not implement anything from this document without going through the harness's own Plan stage and Gate G1 first** (`ai-development-harness.md §1.3, §8`). A task listed here is a *candidate* unit of work — Plan approval is still where scope gets confirmed against the live spec state at the time work actually begins.

---

## 0. How this decomposition works, and its honest limits

`master-specification.md` synthesizes 168 functional requirements, 36 domain rules, 34 business rules, 45 NFRs, ~640 cricket rules, 28 database tables, dozens of API endpoints, and 28 UX screens (`ai-context-pack.md §14`). Turning literally every one of those into its own hand-written task here would produce many hundreds of near-duplicate entries and would not survive one document without drift or error — the same asymmetric-mapping judgment this corpus has applied consistently (`api-specification.md §19.2`, `testing-strategy.md §19.1`, `acceptance-criteria.md §11`) applies here too.

This document therefore does two things, honestly separated:

1. **§4 fully decomposes the MVP-critical foundation** — the database schema, the shared scoring core, the offline event log, and the sync/conflict layer — into 38 concrete, small, dependency-ordered tasks (`TASK-0001`…`TASK-0038`). This is the part of the system with the deepest existing specification detail (`live-scoring.md`'s 15-step pipeline, `data-specification.md`'s 28 tables) and the highest cost of getting wrong first, so it is decomposed in full rather than by pattern.
2. **§5 works one vertical slice end-to-end** (database → shared core → API → UI → test) for a single user-facing capability, showing how a feature actually cuts across the foundation tasks above once they exist.
3. **§6 defines the generation rule** — the exact, repeatable method — for producing the remaining backlog (the other 10 generic CRUD resources, the other 27 UX screens, DLS/dual-scorer/V1+ scope) on demand, with one worked example each, rather than pre-writing hundreds of tasks for scope that isn't being built yet.

§3's epic map shows where every part of the Master Specification lands, decomposed or not, so nothing is silently missing from the plan even where it isn't yet broken into tasks.

---

## 1. Sizing rule — "small enough for one AI development loop"

A task in this backlog satisfies **all** of the following, or it is split further before being listed:

1. **Touches one architectural layer primarily** (schema, or shared-core pipeline stage, or API layer, or one screen) — a task needing schema changes *and* new UI in the same unit is two tasks, linked by Dependencies.
2. **Cites five or fewer requirement/rule IDs directly** (a table's own field list, or one pipeline stage's rule set) — a task needing more is a sign the underlying spec cluster itself needs splitting first.
3. **Has a Definition of Done checkable by the Verify stage's five mechanical checks alone** (`ai-development-harness.md §7`) — no task depends on a judgment call to know if it's finished.
4. **Produces one reviewable diff** small enough that Gate G2 Review (`ai-development-harness.md §8`) is a single sitting, not a multi-day audit.
5. **Its Dependencies list only tasks earlier in this backlog** — no forward references, no circular waits. The backlog is topologically ordered; a task's number is always safe to start once every ID in its Dependencies field shows `Merged` status.

A task that would violate any of these (e.g. "implement the scoring engine") is not a task — it's an epic (§3), and stays unlisted at task grain until broken down.

---

## 2. Task template

Every task in §4 and §5 uses exactly these eleven fields, matching the request that opened this document and remaining compatible with the harness's own `TASK-####` schema (`ai-development-harness.md §4` — `Type`/`Status`/`Approval gate(s)` are implied for every task below: `Type: Feature`, `Status: Drafted`, `Approval gate(s) required: G1+G2` unless stated otherwise).

```
Task ID:                 TASK-####
Requirement IDs:          [the ≤5 IDs this task exists to satisfy]
Goal:                      [one sentence — what exists once this task is done]
Context needed:            [which docs/sections — beyond ai-context-pack.md + ai-development-harness.md, always implicitly loaded]
Dependencies:              [TASK-#### IDs that must be Merged first, or "None — foundation task"]
Inputs:                    [what pre-state/data this task assumes exists]
Expected behavior:         [precise, deterministic description — what the result must do, not how]
Acceptance criteria:       [existing criteria this satisfies, by trace — acceptance-criteria.md has no bare numeric IDs; cite by its §/category/source rule per acceptance-criteria.md §1.3]
Tests required:            [test tier(s), citing testing-strategy.md]
Files/components expected to change: [directories/paths, per repository-structure.md — nothing is created by this backlog itself]
Verification procedure:    [ai-development-harness.md §7's five checks, plus anything task-specific]
```

## 4. Foundation tasks — fully decomposed

### E-01 — Database: Identity & Tenancy

#### TASK-0001 — Migrate `users`, `organizations`, `memberships` tables

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `data-specification.md §3.1–3.3`; `DR-*` identity/tenancy rules (SRS §5); `MINV-*` identity invariants (`domain-model.md`) |
| Goal | The three identity/tenancy tables exist in Postgres exactly as specified — fields, types, PKs, FKs, constraints, indexes, audit fields. |
| Context needed | `data-specification.md §1` (conventions: client-generated UUID identity, audit fields, soft-deletion policy), `§3` |
| Dependencies | None — foundation task |
| Inputs | None (first migration) |
| Expected behavior | `CREATE TABLE` statements matching `data-specification.md §3.1–3.3` field-for-field; `memberships` enforces the org-scoped role FK; soft-deletion applied only where §3 marks it, not uniformly (`data-specification.md §1.7`). |
| Acceptance criteria | The baseline schema-conformance criterion implied by every field in `data-specification.md §3` (no bespoke AC numbering exists for schema fields — trace is the table spec itself, per `acceptance-criteria.md §11`'s stated asymmetry). |
| Tests required | Database tests — schema/constraint conformance (`testing-strategy.md §7`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `database/supabase/migrations/..._create_users.sql`, `..._create_organizations.sql`, `..._create_memberships.sql` |
| Verification procedure | `ai-development-harness.md §7` checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4 (migration applies cleanly, pgTAP schema assertions pass); manual diff against `data-specification.md §3`'s field tables |

#### TASK-0002 — RLS policies for identity & tenancy tables

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `SR-B01` (RLS is the enforcement boundary), `SR-B02` (match-scoped vs org-wide roles), `SR-B05` (cross-tenant isolation) |
| Goal | Every identity/tenancy table has an RLS policy enforcing tenant isolation and role-appropriate access, verified by policy tests. |
| Context needed | `security-specification.md §4` (`SR-B*` cluster) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0001` |
| Inputs | The three tables from `TASK-0001` |
| Expected behavior | A user can read/write only rows their `memberships` entitle them to; no policy grants cross-organization visibility by default (`SR-B05`). |
| Acceptance criteria | `acceptance-criteria.md §10` (Security category) cross-tenant-isolation criteria |
| Tests required | Security tests — RLS policy matrix (`testing-strategy.md §15`), Database tests (`§7`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `database/supabase/migrations/..._create_rls_policies.sql`, `database/supabase/tests/` |
| Verification procedure | pgTAP RLS matrix passes for every actor role defined in `security-specification.md §4.2`; §7 checks 7.2–7.4 |

| Field | Value |

#### TASK-0007 — INSERT-only grants & hash-chain enforcement

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `ADR-08`, `C-4`/`C-9` (`ai-context-pack.md §1` — corrections supersede, tamper-evident record) |
| Goal | No role can `UPDATE` or `DELETE` a `match_events` row; a trigger rejects any insert whose hash doesn't chain from the prior row. |
| Context needed | `data-specification.md §6.1`, `security-specification.md §4` |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0006`, `TASK-0002` (RLS pattern established) |
| Inputs | `match_events` table |
| Expected behavior | `GRANT` statements omit `UPDATE`/`DELETE` for every application role; an insert with a broken hash chain is rejected. |
| Acceptance criteria | `acceptance-criteria.md §10` (Security) append-only-integrity entries |
| Tests required | Security tests (`§15`), Database tests (`§7`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `database/supabase/migrations/..._match_events_grants_and_chain_trigger.sql`, `database/supabase/tests/` |
| Verification procedure | pgTAP test attempting `UPDATE`/`DELETE` and a broken-chain `INSERT`, both asserted rejected; §7 checks 7.2, 7.4 |

#### TASK-0008 — Reference-data versioning & pin enforcement

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `ADR-11` (reference data versioned and pinned per match at creation) |
| Goal | A constraint/trigger prevents a match's pinned `reference_data` version from changing after its first recorded delivery. |
| Context needed | `data-specification.md §5.4`, `ai-context-pack.md C-6/FA-6` (no silent format assumptions) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0005`, `TASK-0006` (needs `match_events` to detect "first delivery recorded") |
| Inputs | `matches`, `reference_data`, `match_events` tables |
| Expected behavior | An `UPDATE` attempting to change `matches.conditions_profile`, `conditions_profile_version`, or `dls_table_version` (§5.1's actual pinned-reference-data fields — this entry originally said `reference_data_id`, a column that doesn't exist; corrected 2026-09-23 during implementation) after any `match_events` row exists for that match is rejected at the database layer. |
| Acceptance criteria | `acceptance-criteria.md §6` (Cricket edge) entries on profile immutability |
| Tests required | Database tests (`§7`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `database/supabase/migrations/..._reference_data_pin_trigger.sql` |
| Verification procedure | pgTAP test attempting the forbidden update and asserting rejection; §7 checks 7.2, 7.4 |

#### TASK-0043 — Batter-replacement end resolution (`§9.5`)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-23, requested explicitly after `TASK-0021`'s report found this scope unclaimed by any existing task — `§21.7`'s `C31`/`C36–C38` cases test it, but neither `TASK-0021` (mode-validity) nor `TASK-0022` (zero-vs-preserve runs) actually covers it. Numbered `0043`, appended after `TASK-0042`, per the "sequential, never reused" rule.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `live-scoring.md §9.5` (batter-replacement end resolution) |
| Goal | Given a dismissal, determine which crease end the incoming batter occupies and which end the surviving batter ends up at. |
| Context needed | `live-scoring.md §9.5` |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0021` |
| Inputs | A dismissal mode, the dismissed batter's crease end at the start of the delivery, the declared `endVacated`, and (for `RUN_OUT` only) `crossedBeforeDismissal` |
| Expected behavior | Non-`RUN_OUT`: the new batter takes exactly `endVacated`, the survivor stays at the other end. `RUN_OUT`, not crossed: survivor stays at their original end, new batter takes the declared `endVacated`. `RUN_OUT`, crossed: the survivor and dismissed batter have swapped ends (matching how batters physically swap on any completed run) — survivor ends up at the dismissed batter's original end, new batter takes the survivor's original end. |
| Acceptance criteria | `live-scoring.md §21.7` `C31` (non-`RUN_OUT` baseline), `C36–C38` (the three `RUN_OUT` sub-cases, including the not-crossed/crossed distinction) |
| Tests required | Domain/conformance tests (`§4`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/.../model/` (`CreaseEnd`), `.../pipeline/` |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.2, 7.4–7.5; all of `C31`/`C36–C38` pass |

#### TASK-0022 — Always-zero-runs vs. may-carry-runs dismissal resolution

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `live-scoring.md §9` (the corrected always-zero-runs set `{BOWLED, CAUGHT, LBW, STUMPED, HIT_BALL_TWICE}` vs. may-carry-runs set `{RUN_OUT, HIT_WICKET, OBSTRUCTING_THE_FIELD}`) |
| Goal | A dismissal in the always-zero-runs set forces `ranRuns = 0` regardless of input; a may-carry-runs dismissal preserves whatever runs were legitimately run before the dismissal. |
| Context needed | `live-scoring.md §9` (including the caught-and-bowled = `CAUGHT` with `fielderIds=[bowlerId]` clarifying note) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0021`, `TASK-0019` |
| Inputs | A dismissal mode + the delivery's `RunEvent` set |
| Expected behavior | A `RUN_OUT` on the non-striker's end during a legitimate second run preserves the completed first run; a `CAUGHT` dismissal zeroes any attempted run in flight. |
| Acceptance criteria | `live-scoring.md §21.7`; `live-scoring.md §22` `EX-*` worked examples involving run-outs |
| Tests required | Domain/conformance tests (`§4`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `shared/src/commonMain/kotlin/.../pipeline/` |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.2, 7.4–7.5; the specific `EX-*` examples involving mid-run dismissals pass exactly |

## 5. Worked vertical slice — "Create a match" (`UX-04`)

Shows how one user-facing capability cuts across foundation tasks once they exist, and how E-19/E-20/E-22 (not epic-decomposed above) get sliced when their turn comes.

#### TASK-0039 — ~~API: `POST /matches`~~ API: `PUT /matches/{id}` (generic CRUD create)

**Corrected during implementation (2026-09-24):** `api-specification.md §10.1` states outright "there is no server-assigning `POST /{resource}` for any table in this section" — every resource, matches included, uses `PUT /{resource}/{id}` with a **client-supplied** id; `data-specification.md §5.1` independently confirms `matches.id` is "Client-generated — a match is always created offline-first." Implemented as `PUT`, not `POST`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | SRS cluster B (Match Setup); `api-specification.md §10` (generic CRUD pattern) |
| Goal | An authenticated, authorized request with a valid match-creation payload creates a `matches` row and returns it. |
| Context needed | ~~`api-specification.md §9` (validation layers, error registry)~~ **corrected:** `§4` (Validation, the three-layer model) and `§5` (Error codes, the registry) — §9 is "Versioning," unrelated. `§10`. |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0005`, `TASK-0014` |
| Inputs | A valid create-match payload per `api-specification.md §10`'s generic schema |
| Expected behavior | ~~A payload missing a required field returns the exact `422` per §9's registry~~ **corrected:** per `§4.1`'s own three-layer table, a missing/malformed field is a **schema** validation failure (`400`), not a business-rule one (`422`) — `422` is reserved for a well-formed payload violating a domain rule (e.g. this table's own `CK`: `home_team_id <> away_team_id`). A valid payload returns `201` with the created row, including server-assigned audit fields. |
| Acceptance criteria | `acceptance-criteria.md §3–§5` (Normal/Boundary/Invalid) entries traced to SRS cluster B |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/` (or generic CRUD handler if not command-specific), `database/supabase/functions/` (thin entrypoint) |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5 |

#### TASK-0040 — Web: `UX-04 Create Match` screen

**Corrected during implementation (2026-09-24):** `ux-specification.md UX-04`'s own Inputs are `Match label, guest-vs-organization toggle, template selector, quick-pick format` only — it does **not** collect `home_team_id`/`away_team_id`/`match_timezone`, all `NOT NULL` on `data-specification.md §5.1`'s `matches` table (team selection is `UX-06`, timezone is `UX-05`, both later screens). "Submitting the form offline creates the match" (this task's own Expected Behavior) therefore cannot mean calling `TASK-0039`'s `createMatch()` from this screen — doing so would require fabricating values this screen never collects. Implemented as producing a local `DraftMatch` (client-generated id, everything UX-04 itself gathers) instead; wiring the full multi-screen `UX-04→09` flow into one eventual `PUT /matches/{id}` call is a genuine, flagged open integration question for later, not resolved by guessing here.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `ux-specification.md` `UX-04` |
| Goal | A scorer can create a match from the web app, offline or online, reaching `UX-05 Match Setup` on success. |
| Context needed | `ux-specification.md`'s `UX-04` entry (Purpose/Inputs/Actions/Validation/States/Error handling/Empty states/Offline behavior/Accessibility) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0039`, `TASK-0034` (offline command execution — match creation must work with zero connectivity per `C-1`) |
| Inputs | The API contract from `TASK-0039` |
| Expected behavior | ~~Submitting the form offline creates the match locally and queues the event, per `TASK-0034`'s model~~ **corrected:** UX-04 itself only collects label/ownership/template/format, not the full `matches` row — it produces a local draft; actually creating the row (queuing the event) happens once the remaining required fields are known, a later integration point. The screen does not block on network either way. |
| Acceptance criteria | `acceptance-criteria.md §3` (Normal) plus `§7` (Offline) entries for this screen |
| Tests required | Web tests (`§13`), the `UX-04→UX-05` step of the relevant E2E workflow (`§14`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `apps/web/src/screens/UX-04-create-match/` |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5; manual offline-mode walkthrough |

## 6. Generation rule for the remaining backlog

### 6.1 Generic CRUD / command endpoint tasks (E-19, E-20, E-21)

For each of the 10 remaining generic-CRUD resources (`api-specification.md §10`) and each match-lifecycle command (`§11`) not yet covered: **one task per resource-operation pair** (e.g. `TASK-#### — API: PATCH /teams/{id}`), using exactly `TASK-0039`'s template — Requirement IDs from the resource's SRS cluster, Dependencies on that resource's schema task (§4) plus `TASK-0014`, Expected behavior stated as the validation/response contract from ~~`api-specification.md §9–§10`~~ **corrected (found while minting `TASK-0044`, the same error `TASK-0039` already caught in its own field text): `§4` (Validation) and `§5` (Error codes) — `§9` is "Versioning," unrelated.** `§10` for the CRUD shape itself. Tests required = API tests (`§6`). A command endpoint (§11) additionally depends on the shared-core task(s) implementing the command's effect (e.g. a "record delivery" command endpoint depends on all of E-08–E-14).

#### TASK-0044 — API: `matches` update (`PUT /matches/{id}`, existing id)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-24 — the first task generated by `§6.1`'s own rule, using `TASK-0039`'s template exactly. Completes `TASK-0039`'s own explicitly-flagged gap: "an update — existing id, `row_version` check — is a natural extension not built here." Chosen over starting a fresh resource because it finishes `matches`' CRUD story before moving on, reuses `TASK-0039`'s already-built `MatchStore`/`MatchRow`/error-constructor types directly, and is the first task in this backlog to genuinely exercise `api-specification.md §4.1`'s third validation layer — state-dependent/`409` optimistic concurrency — completing coverage of all three layers (`400`/`422`/`409`) across the two `matches` tasks together.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | SRS cluster B (Match Setup); `api-specification.md §10` (generic CRUD pattern, the update half) |
| Goal | An authenticated, authorized `PUT /matches/{id}` request against an existing match, with a current `row_version`, updates the row; a stale `row_version` is rejected, never silently overwritten. |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §4` (Validation, the three-layer model — this task's own focus is the third, state-dependent layer), `§5` (Error codes), `§10.1` (`PUT` create-or-update semantics), `§10.2`'s `matches` row (write-once-then-locked fields: `home_xi`/`away_xi`/`toss_*`/`conditions_profile*`) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0039` |
| Inputs | An existing `matches` row (from `TASK-0039`'s create path) plus an update payload carrying the `row_version` last read |
| Expected behavior | A `PUT` with the current `row_version` updates the row and increments `row_version`; a `PUT` with a stale (non-matching) `row_version` is rejected `409 concurrency/stale-version`, the stored row left untouched; a `PUT` attempting to change a write-once-then-locked field after it's already set is rejected `422 validation/business-rule` (`BR-017`, `MINV-05`), distinct from the stale-version case. |
| Acceptance criteria | `acceptance-criteria.md §3–§5` (Normal/Boundary/Invalid) entries traced to SRS cluster B, extended to the update case |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/matches.ts` (extended, not a new module — the update path belongs alongside `createMatch`) |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5; a stale-`row_version` update is rejected with the stored row provably unchanged; a valid update is confirmed to increment `row_version` by exactly 1 |

#### TASK-0093 — API: `organizations` CRUD (`PUT` create, `PUT` update, `GET` list, `GET` one)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-28, the second `§6.1`-generated task, following `TASK-0044`'s own next-pick recommendation once the entire `§6.2` UX-screen decomposition closed out — `organizations` chosen first among the 8 remaining generic-CRUD resources because it's the tenancy root every other resource (`memberships`, `teams`, `matches`) FKs into. **A deliberate sizing departure from `TASK-0039`/`0044`'s own precedent, flagged explicitly:** those two tasks split `matches`' create and update into separate tasks because `matches` has real per-operation complexity (frozen-field tracking, a `CK` business rule) that justified the split. `organizations` (`data-specification.md §3.2`) has **no request-shape deviations from storage at all** (`api-specification.md §10.2`'s own table says so explicitly — the deviations column is empty) and no business-rule layer beyond schema presence — genuinely boilerplate CRUD with nothing to size-split. Bundling `create`+`update`+`list`+`get-one` into one task keeps it "small enough for one AI development loop" in substance (it reuses `TASK-0039`/`0044`'s already-built error-constructor types directly and adds no new validation complexity), even though it covers more HTTP operations than either `matches` task did. **`DELETE` is deliberately excluded, not silently built with an invented policy:** `data-specification.md §3.2` states outright, "Soft deletion: not defined in this iteration — organizations are not expected to be deleted; revisit if a decommissioning flow is added," matching the open item `DSQ-2` in that same document's own table. `api-specification.md §10.1`'s `DELETE` contract requires following "that resource's `data-specification.md §1.7` policy exactly" — there is no policy to follow yet, and per `FA-9` ("it's fine to invent an endpoint/field/schema that 'seems missing'" is a false assumption — a real gap is a spec change, not a silent addition), inventing a hard-delete-or-soft-deactivate behavior here would be exactly that. `DELETE /organizations/{id}` stays unbuilt until `DSQ-2` resolves. **A real filterable-fields gap found, resolved conservatively:** `api-specification.md §6`'s own "Filterable fields" sentence lists `teams`/`players` → `organizationId, name_search` and several other resources, but omits `organizations` entirely, despite `data-specification.md §3.2` giving `organizations.name` its own dedicated trigram index explicitly "for admin search" — the same index shape `teams`/`players` already use to support their own `name_search` filter. Implemented `name_search` here anyway, reasoning from the index's own stated purpose rather than inventing a new one; flagged as a spec omission, not silently worked around.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `data-specification.md §3.2` (`organizations` table); `api-specification.md §10.1/§10.2` (the generic CRUD pattern; the `organizations` row, no shape deviations), `§6`/`§7` (pagination/filtering); SRS `FR-005` (organizations/tenants, Must·P1), `BR-001`, `SEC-005` (dependency) |
| Goal | `PUT /organizations/{id}` creates a new organization (client-supplied id, per `§10.1`) or updates an existing one (with a `row_version` check, `409` on staleness); `GET /organizations/{id}` fetches one, `404` identical whether truly absent or RLS-hidden; `GET /organizations` returns a keyset-paginated, `name_search`-filterable list. |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §4` (Validation, three-layer model), `§5` (Error codes), `§6` (Pagination — keyset, `after`/`limit`/`nextCursor`/`hasMore`), `§7` (Filtering — `name_search`), `§10.1/§10.2` |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0039`/`TASK-0044` (the `matches` CRUD precedent this task's own shape and error-constructor reuse follow directly) |
| Inputs | A create/update payload (`id`, `name`, `branding?`), a `row_version` on update, list query params (`after`, `limit`, `name_search`) |
| Expected behavior | `createOrganization` schema-validates (`id`, `name` required) then inserts with `rowVersion: 1`; `updateOrganization` 404s on a missing id, schema-fails on a missing `rowVersion`, `409`s on a stale one, else applies a partial update (fields absent from the payload unchanged) and increments `rowVersion` by exactly 1; `getOrganization` 404s identically whether absent or RLS-hidden; `listOrganizations` returns at most `limit` (default 50, max 200) rows ordered by `id`, `name_search` filtering case-insensitively by substring, with a `nextCursor`/`hasMore` envelope per `§6`. |
| Acceptance criteria | No `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster case cites `organizations` CRUD directly (checked) — grounded in `FR-005`'s own two acceptance lines (tenant data isolation; an org can own teams/competitions/matches once created) and `api-specification.md §4/§6/§7`'s own contract text. |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/organizations.ts` (new module, mirroring `matches.ts`'s own structure) |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vitest run` genuinely executed |

#### TASK-0094 — API: `teams` CRUD (`PUT` create, `PUT` update, `GET` list, `GET` one, `DELETE`)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-28, the third `§6.1`-generated task — `teams` picked next per the user's own direction ("what is next" → `AskUserQuestion`), since it feeds directly into the already-built Match Setup/Team Selection screens (`UX-05`-`07`) which have so far only had `shared/`'s pure pipeline logic and a stub core, never a real backend resource to read/write against. **Unlike `organizations`, `teams` genuinely earns a `DELETE`, not excluded:** `data-specification.md §4.1`'s own "Soft deletion" line gives `teams` a real, fully-specified three-part policy — "not applicable [no soft-delete] — a team with match history is never deleted; an unused ad-hoc team may be hard-deleted by its creator only (no historical references exist yet)" — a defined policy to follow exactly, unlike `organizations`' genuinely-undefined `DSQ-2` gap. Implemented as two gates: (1) a business-rule check that the team has no match history (`422` if it does — the same "never delete a row with historical references" principle `data-specification.md §1.7`'s policy-1 embodies for every such table), and (2) an authorization check that the caller is the team's own `createdBy` (`403` otherwise, not merely "any admin" — the spec's own "by its creator only" is more specific than a general write-role check). **No injected "has match history" query exists anywhere in this backend layer yet** (no `matches`↔`teams` join has been built) — modeled as a `TeamStore.hasMatchHistory(id)` method a real adapter would implement via a query against `matches`/`squad_members`; the in-memory test store exposes a settable marker so tests can exercise both branches, flagged as the same kind of test-double simplification `MatchStore.update`'s own doc comment (`TASK-0044`) already established a precedent for. **Filterable fields, no gap this time:** `api-specification.md §6`'s own sentence explicitly names `teams` → `organizationId, name_search` — both implemented directly, no spec-gap flag needed (unlike `organizations`' finding). **Create/update themselves are still boilerplate** — `§10.2`'s own table lists no request-shape deviations for `teams` either — so this task's real complexity is entirely in `DELETE`'s two-gate check, not in create/update.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `data-specification.md §4.1` (`teams` table, including its own fully-specified soft-deletion policy); `api-specification.md §10.1/§10.2` (`teams` row, no create/update shape deviations), `§6`/`§7` (pagination/filtering — `organizationId`, `name_search`, both spec-named); SRS `FR-030` (team squad list, Must·P1, traces to discovery `FR-031`) |
| Goal | `PUT /teams/{id}` creates (client-supplied id, nullable `organizationId` for a guest/ad-hoc team) or updates (`row_version`-checked) a team; `GET /teams/{id}` fetches one; `GET /teams` returns a keyset-paginated, `organizationId`/`name_search`-filterable list; `DELETE /teams/{id}` hard-deletes only when the team has no match history AND the caller is its own creator — otherwise refused, never a silent no-op or a wrong-policy soft-deactivate. |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §4` (Validation), `§5` (Error codes), `§6`/`§7` (pagination/filtering), `§10.1/§10.2`; `data-specification.md §4.1`'s own Soft-deletion line, read verbatim rather than assumed |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0093` (the `organizations` CRUD precedent this task's create/update/list/get-one shape follows directly) |
| Inputs | A create/update payload (`id`, `organizationId?`, `name`, `canonicalRef?`), a `row_version` on update, list query params (`after`, `limit`, `organizationId`, `nameSearch`), a delete request (`id` + caller's `actorRef`) |
| Expected behavior | `createTeam`/`updateTeam`/`getTeam`/`listTeams` mirror `organizations.ts`'s own shape exactly, with `organizationId`/`canonicalRef` added as nullable fields; `deleteTeam` 404s on a missing id, `422`s (business-rule) if `store.hasMatchHistory(id)` is true, `403`s (forbidden) if `actorRef !== existing.createdBy`, else removes the row and returns success — checked in that order (existence, then history, then authorship) so the caller always learns the most specific reason first. |
| Acceptance criteria | No `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster case cites `teams` CRUD directly (checked) — grounded in `FR-030`'s own acceptance line (a squad's players become selectable for the XI once it exists) and `data-specification.md §4.1`'s own Soft-deletion text, read verbatim. |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/teams.ts` (new module, mirroring `organizations.ts`'s own structure, plus the two-gate `deleteTeam`) |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vitest run` genuinely executed; both `deleteTeam` refusal branches (history, authorship) and the success path are exercised |

#### TASK-0095 — API: `memberships` CRUD (`PUT` create, `PUT` update — `roles` only, `GET` list, `GET` one; no `DELETE`)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, the fourth `§6.1`-generated task — `memberships` picked next (the RBAC join table `UX-28`'s own role-editor already assumes exists) per the recommendation left at the end of `TASK-0094`'s own session. **A real, spec-mandated field exclusion, not a sizing shortcut:** `api-specification.md §10.2`'s own table says, for `memberships` specifically, "`status` is response-only on create (always starts `ACTIVE`); use `§11.3` to deactivate, not a `PUT` with `status=DEACTIVATED` directly, so a deactivation is always explicit and auditable." Both `CreateMembershipPayload` and `UpdateMembershipPayload` therefore omit `status` entirely from their own TypeScript shape — not merely "ignored if present" but structurally absent, the same precedent `matches.ts` (`TASK-0039`) already set for its own response-only fields (`state`/`result`). **`DELETE` is deliberately NOT built here either, for a related but distinct reason:** `data-specification.md §3.3`'s own Soft-deletion line is policy 2 (`status = DEACTIVATED`, never a row delete) — meaning a generic `DELETE` on this resource, if built, would have to perform exactly the same `status` transition `§11.3`'s own dedicated endpoint already owns; `api-specification.md §11.3`'s own note calls that endpoint out as "deliberately a dedicated endpoint... so deactivation is always an explicit, auditable act, never an incidental side effect of an unrelated [call]" — which reads as being about more than just `PUT`. Rather than duplicate deactivation logic across two endpoints (this task's `DELETE` and a future `§11.3` task) with the risk of them drifting apart, `DELETE /memberships/{id}` is left to that dedicated future task entirely, same as `§6.1`'s own rule distinguishes "generic CRUD" from "match-lifecycle command" as separate task categories. **A real composite-uniqueness business rule implemented, not overlooked:** `§3.3`'s own `UQ: (user_id, organization_id)` constraint means `createMembership` must reject a second membership for the same user+org pair under a different `id`, not merely reject a reused `id` — a distinct check from `organizations`'/`teams`' own "id already exists" case, added as its own business-rule (`422`) path. **A real validation question resolved by reading the foundation doc directly, not invented:** `§3.3`'s own field table cites "the 12 additive roles (`foundation §4`)" for the `roles: text[]` field — read `product-foundation.md §4`'s own role table directly (Platform Admin, Organization Admin, Competition/Tournament Organizer, Team Manager/Captain, Head Scorer, Assistant/Co-Scorer, Umpire, Commentator, Statistician/Analyst, Player, Viewer/Spectator, Guest) and validated `roles` against exactly those 12, normalized to the `HEAD_SCORER`/`ASSISTANT_SCORER`/`UMPIRE` naming `domain-model.md`'s own `VO-SIGNATURE` already uses as precedent for the other nine. **Flagged, not silently resolved:** `system-architecture.md §3.9` describes "Match-level roles (Head Scorer, Assistant Scorer)" as "assignments on the match within its org," which could be read as implying those two roles live on a per-match assignment record (`match_officials`) rather than this flat `memberships.roles` array — but `data-specification.md §3.3`'s own field definition is the schema's actual source of truth and states plainly that all 12 roles live in this one field; followed the schema table over the architecture doc's descriptive gloss, the same "spec's own authoritative text over paraphrase" rule applied all session, and flagged the tension rather than silently picking one reading. `GUEST` is included in the valid set despite being a poor practical fit for a `memberships` row (device-local, no account, per the foundation table's own "Cannot: sync, share, or collaborate until an account is created") — kept in because the spec literally says "the 12," not "11 of the 12," and no textual exception is given.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `data-specification.md §3.3` (`memberships` table — the `status`-response-only deviation, the `UQ(user_id, organization_id)` constraint, the 12-role `roles` field); `api-specification.md §10.1/§10.2` (the `status` deviation note), `§6`/`§7` (pagination/filtering — `organizationId`/`userId`/`status`, all three spec-named); `product-foundation.md §4` (the 12-role table); SRS `FR-007` (role assignment/revocation, Must·P1, clean discovery match), `SEC-005` (additive RBAC model, Must·P1) |
| Goal | `PUT /memberships/{id}` creates (client-supplied id; `status` always starts `ACTIVE`, not settable) or updates (`row_version`-checked; `roles` the only mutable field besides the fixed identity pair) a membership; `GET /memberships/{id}` fetches one; `GET /memberships` returns a keyset-paginated, `organizationId`/`userId`/`status`-filterable list. stale `row_version` is `409`" contract cannot apply here at all — `addSquadMember` is therefore a pure idempotent upsert of `role_hint` (the only mutable, non-key field), never a `409`, consistent **`DELETE` is a plain, ungated hard delete, no business-rule check needed:** `§4.3`'s own Soft-deletion line states plainly that removing a squad-list entry "never orphans a historical reference" (the historical fact of having played lives in `batter_card_lines`/`bowler_card_lines`, not here) — unlike `teams`' two-gate `deleteTeam`, there is nothing to check beyond existence. **No filterable-fields gap, and no filterable-fields finding needed at all:** `§6`'s own "Filterable fields" sentence doesn't mention `squad_members` — checked, and this is structurally correct, not an omission like `organizations`' real gap: `teamId` is already a path parameter (`/teams/{teamId}/squad-members`), not a query filter, since this resource is inherently scoped to one team by its own nested URL shape. **No cross-resource FK-existence check added** — consistent with `organizations`/`teams`/`memberships`/`players`' own established precedent of not verifying a referenced id (`organizationId`, etc.) actually exists in its sibling store; this in-memory layer models validation, not real FK enforcement, same boundary throughout.)*

#### TASK-0099 — API: `match_officials` CRUD (composite three-key `PUT` add, `GET` list, `GET` one, `DELETE`; no update)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, the eighth `§6.1`-generated task — `match_officials` picked next, completing the pair recommended at the end of `TASK-0098`'s own session. **A genuine URL-shape vs. PK-shape tension, flagged and resolved deliberately, not silently assumed away:** `data-specification.md §5.3`'s own PK is the **three-part** composite `(match_id, official_id, role)`, but `api-specification.md §10.2`'s own deviation note only says `{id}` in the generic pattern is `{officialId}` under `/matches/{matchId}/officials` — leaving `role` with no stated place in the URL at all. Since `role` is a genuine part of the identifying key (the same official could, schema-wise, hold two different roles on the same match as two distinct rows), this task's own module keys every operation by the full `(matchId, officialId, role)` triple, treating `role` as a required field the caller must always supply alongside the path's own `officialId` (whether that lands as a query parameter or request-body field in a real HTTP binding is a framework-layer detail out of this module's own scope) — rather than silently assuming one official can hold at most one role per match, an assumption the schema itself does not make. **A real consequence of the table's own "no update fields" prose, taken even further than `squad_members`':** unlike `squad_members` (which still had `role_hint` to upsert), `match_officials` has `created_at`/`created_by` as its only non-key fields — genuinely nothing mutable exists once a triple is created. "Reassignment is a remove+add" (the table's own words) is therefore not a design choice this module works around, it's the ONLY possible way to change an official's role, since `role` is part of the key: there is no update operation here at all, not even a trivial one. **A real, narrowly-scoped business rule implemented, reasoned carefully rather than over- or under-built:** `domain-model.md`'s own `VO-MATCH-OFFICIALS`/`OFCL-003` states "exactly one `HEAD` [scorer]" — a maximum-count constraint this module CAN enforce incrementally (reject a second `HEAD_SCORER` assignment on the same match), implemented as `addMatchOfficial`'s own business-rule check. **`OFCL-001/011`'s minimum-count rules ("≥ 1 on-field umpire, 2 for ICC profiles"; "≥ 1 scorer") are deliberately NOT enforced here** — they are panel-*readiness* gates checkable only once the whole roster is assembled (checking them per-add would make it impossible to ever add the very first official), and `OFCL-001/011`'s own "2 for ICC profiles" clause depends on which playing-conditions profile the match uses, a context this generic CRUD layer has no visibility into — the same "no visibility into match state" boundary `TASK-0044`'s own `validateFrozenFields` already flagged for `matches.ts`. **A real schema-vs-domain-model enum mismatch found, not fixed (out of this task's own scope):** `§5.3`'s own `role` enum is `{UMPIRE, THIRD_UMPIRE, REFEREE, HEAD_SCORER, ASSISTANT_SCORER}` — five values — while `domain-model.md`'s own `VO-MATCH-OFFICIALS` names a `fourthUmpire?` slot with no corresponding `FOURTH_UMPIRE` table-level role value; `validateRole` here validates against exactly the table's own five, flagging the mismatch rather than silently inventing a sixth enum value the schema doesn't define. **`DELETE` is a plain, ungated hard delete** — `§5.3`'s own Soft-deletion line: "the official's own historical involvement remains visible via the read-model card lines'... provenance regardless of whether the assignment row still exists," so nothing is orphaned by removal.)*

|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `data-specification.md §5.3` (`match_officials` table — the three-part composite PK, "no update fields" prose, the ordinary-hard-delete policy); `api-specification.md §10.1/§10.2` (the `{officialId}`-only URL deviation note, and this task's own resolution of its tension with the real three-part PK); `domain-model.md` `VO-MATCH-OFFICIALS`/`OFCL-003` (exactly-one-`HEAD`-scorer, enforced), `OFCL-001/011` (minimum-count panel-readiness, explicitly NOT enforced here, see mint note); SRS `FR-025` (match officials, Should·P1, same citation `officials` already used) |
| Goal | `PUT /matches/{matchId}/officials/{officialId}` (with `role` supplied alongside) adds an official-role assignment to a match — idempotent on the exact triple, never a `409` (no `row_version` exists), rejected (`422`) only if the assignment would be a second `HEAD_SCORER` on the same match; `GET .../officials/{officialId}` fetches one exact triple (`role` required to disambiguate); `GET /matches/{matchId}/officials` lists the whole panel for one match; `DELETE .../officials/{officialId}` (with `role`) removes the exact triple outright, no gate. |

#### TASK-0100 — API: `reference_data` CRUD (`PUT` publish-new-version-only, `GET` list, `GET` one, `GET` latest; no update, no `DELETE`)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, the ninth and final `§6.1`-generated generic-CRUD task — `data-specification.md §5.4`'s `reference_data` table, which backs `api-specification.md §10.2`'s "Condition templates" row (`kind=CONDITIONS_PROFILE`) and, incidentally, its two sibling kinds (`DLS_TABLE`, `APP_CONFIG`) the same shared table also holds — building the real table faithfully rather than artificially narrowing to one `kind`, the same reasoning `teams.ts` already applied by not restricting itself to only ad-hoc teams despite `FR-033` naming that case specifically. **A genuine URL-shape vs. schema-shape mismatch, the second one found in this cluster (after `match_officials`' three-part-PK tension) and the most structurally significant yet, flagged rather than silently resolved:** `api-specification.md §10.2`'s own path for this resource is `/organizations/{orgId}/condition-templates` — a nested, org-scoped URL — but `data-specification.md §5.4`'s own field table has **no `organization_id` column at all**; `reference_data` is a genuinely global table (PK `(kind, version)`, no org scoping whatsoever), consistent with its own prose: "devices cache a pinned snapshot" of a platform-wide reference, and `matches.conditions_profile_version`/`dls_table_version` pin against a single shared, versioned sequence, not a per-org one. The `{orgId}` segment in the URL is very likely a stale vestige from an earlier, more org-scoped design never reconciled with `§5.4`'s own final schema — this module implements the real, global shape (`kind`+`version` as the identity, no `organizationId` field invented to match the URL, per `FA-9`). **A second, related tension on WHO may publish, also flagged, not resolved:** `§10.2`'s own deviation note says "Publishing a template is platform/org-admin only," but `§5.4`'s own field-level note restricts `published_by` to "platform-admin" specifically (`system-architecture.md §3.9`) — the narrower of the two; this module accepts any `publishedBy` actor reference without a role check at all, consistent with `ai-context-pack.md FA-7` ("client-side/command-layer permission checks are sufficient authorization" is a false assumption — the real boundary is RLS, not this layer), the same boundary every other `§6.1` module has already respected by never checking org-admin/platform-admin status directly. **The most literal, strictest immutability model built this cluster:** `§5.4`'s own text — "a published `(kind, version)` row is never updated in place — a change is a new `version`" — means there is no update operation **at all**, and `§10.2`'s own text is explicit that a `PUT` against an already-published `(kind, version)` is unconditionally `409 state/invalid-transition`, **"not a silent overwrite"** — read literally as allowing no idempotency exception even for a byte-identical resubmission, unlike `§8.3`'s general "`PUT` is naturally idempotent" framing every other resource in this backlog has relied on; `publishReferenceData` therefore requires the caller's own supplied `version` to be exactly `currentHighest + 1` for that `kind`, rejecting both an already-existing version (`409`) and a version that skips ahead (`422`, a genuine gap in the monotonic sequence) — the strictest of the three because the spec's own wording leaves no stated exception. **`DELETE` is not merely excluded for lack of a defined policy (`organizations`' `DSQ-2` shape) — it is explicitly, permanently ruled out:** `§5.4`'s own Soft-deletion line reads "none — every historical version must remain resolvable for as long as any match still pins it," the strongest exclusion found this session (stronger than "undefined," stronger than "owned by a future command" — this is "never, by design"). **A real, schema-grounded `getLatest` operation added, not invented:** `§5.4`'s own `IX: kind` note states its purpose explicitly — "to fetch the latest quickly" — directly implying a dedicated latest-version lookup per `kind`, built as `getLatestReferenceData`.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `data-specification.md §5.4` (`reference_data` table — composite `(kind, version)` PK, no `organization_id`, the absolute-immutability and no-soft-deletion-ever text, the `IX: kind` "fetch latest" note); `api-specification.md §10.1/§10.2` (the `organizations`-nested URL this task's own mint note flags as inconsistent with `§5.4`'s genuinely global schema; the platform-vs-org-admin publisher tension; `§5.2`'s own `state/invalid-transition` (409) registry entry, which had no constructor yet in `backend/src/authz/errors.ts` — added here as `invalidTransitionError`, distinct from `staleVersionError`'s `concurrency/stale-version`); SRS `BR-032` (reference-data versioning, Must·P1 — already cited once this session, for `UX-28`'s own reference-data-publish Action). **A third citation-correctness finding, this time inside `api-specification.md` itself, not a UX/data-spec cross-reference:** `§10.2`'s own table row cites `FR-016`/`FR-028` for this resource — read directly, `FR-016` is "Create an ad-hoc match" and `FR-028` is "Minimum-overs-for-result configuration," neither of which relates to publishing a condition-profile template at all; not included in this task's own Requirement IDs as validated citations, flagged as a likely mis-citation in `api-specification.md`'s own table rather than silently inherited. |
| Goal | `PUT /condition-templates` (conceptually; the real identity is `kind`+`version`, not a single resource id, per this task's own URL-shape finding) publishes a new version for a `kind` only if the supplied `version` is exactly one past the current highest for that `kind` — never an update, never an overwrite; `GET` one fetches an exact `(kind, version)`; `GET` list returns every version for a `kind` (or all kinds); `GET` latest returns the highest-version row for a `kind`, per `§5.4`'s own `IX` note. No `DELETE` exists at all — explicitly, permanently ruled out by `§5.4`'s own Soft-deletion text. |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §4` (Validation), `§5` (Error codes), `§10.1/§10.2`; `data-specification.md §5.4`, read verbatim for its own composite-PK/no-org-scope/absolute-immutability/never-deleted text |
| Dependencies | None on prior `§6.1` tasks' own shape — this resource's identity (`kind`+`version`) is structurally unlike every other resource built so far; grounded independently from `§5.4` itself |
| Inputs | `kind` (one of `CONDITIONS_PROFILE`/`DLS_TABLE`/`APP_CONFIG`), `version` (caller-supplied, must be `currentHighest + 1`), `payload` (the versioned content, opaque `jsonb`), `publishedBy` |
| Expected behavior | `publishReferenceData` schema-validates (`kind` one of the three, `version`/`payload`/`publishedBy` required), rejects a `version` that already exists (`409`, "not a silent overwrite," no idempotency exception even on an identical resubmission), rejects a `version` that isn't exactly `currentHighest + 1` for that `kind` (`422`, a gap in the monotonic sequence), else inserts a new, permanent row; `getReferenceData`/`listReferenceData` key/filter by `kind` (+`version` for `get`); `getLatestReferenceData` returns the highest-version row for a `kind`, `404` if none published yet. |
| Acceptance criteria | No `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster case cites `reference_data`/`condition-templates` CRUD directly (checked) — grounded in `§5.4`'s own field/PK/immutability/Soft-deletion text and `§10.2`'s own deviation note, both read verbatim. |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/referenceData.ts` (new module — composite `(kind, version)` key, publish-only, no update/delete, a dedicated `getLatest`) |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vitest run` genuinely executed; the already-published-version rejection (`409`), the sequence-gap rejection (`422`), and `getLatest` across multiple published versions are all explicitly exercised |

#### TASK-0101 — API: `deactivateMember` command (`POST /organizations/{orgId}/memberships/{membershipId}/deactivate`)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, the first `§11` match-lifecycle command task — `§6.1`'s own generation rule already covers this category explicitly ("A command endpoint (§11) additionally depends on the shared-core task(s) implementing the command's effect"), so no new backlog subsection is needed, just continuing under `§6.1`'s own umbrella. `deactivateMember` picked first per the user's own direction (`AskUserQuestion`) once `§6.1`'s generic-CRUD list closed out — the simplest of the six P1 `§11` commands, and `memberships.ts`'s own doc comment (`TASK-0095`) already explicitly deferred this exact endpoint here. **A fourth instance of the discovery-vs-SRS citation-namespace collision, the richest variant yet — a true same-number-different-meaning collision on top of the renumbering:** `api-specification.md §11.3`'s own Trace line cites `FR-009, BR-024` — but SRS `BR-024` is "Toss determines initial innings order," wholly unrelated to member deactivation; the REAL SRS entry is `BR-023` ("Deactivated-member authorship retained," Must·P1), which traces to **discovery** `BR-024`+`BR-025` *(consolidated)* — so `§11.3` cited the discovery-level number directly, AND that same number happens to independently mean something completely unrelated at the SRS level, the worst-case combination of the two collision shapes found separately so far (`UX-28`'s clean discovery-vs-SRS renumbering; `UX-22`'s same-number-different-meaning). `BR-023` is used instead, as the real, clean citation. **The first task in this backlog to need `§8`'s own Idempotency-Key mechanism, not `row_version`:** `§11.3`'s own Request shape is literally `{}` (no body fields at all) — no `row_version` to check, confirming `§8.3`'s own framing that command/RPC endpoints (`§11`) use the Idempotency-Key mechanism (`§8.1`/`§8.2`) instead of the `row_version` optimistic-concurrency check every `§6.1` resource has relied on until now. Implemented per `§8.2`'s own precise behavior: a request presenting a key that already produced a **successful** result returns that **identical** prior result (same outcome object, not recomputed); a key that previously **failed** (e.g., a missing membership) is processed fresh next time, never cached — `§8.2`'s own words, "a failed attempt never 'poisons' the key." **An honest scope note, not silently assumed:** `§8.1`'s own `[DEFAULT]` 24-hour retention window for an idempotency key is not modeled here — the in-memory `IdempotencyStore` test double retains keys indefinitely, the same simplification every other in-memory store in this backlog already makes about real persistence; a production adapter owns the TTL. **A real idempotent-domain-state decision, reasoned rather than guessed:** re-deactivating an already-`DEACTIVATED` membership (via a genuinely different Idempotency-Key, not a replayed one) is treated as a safe no-op returning the current row unchanged, not an error — no spec text says a second deactivation should fail, and the resulting state is identical either way. **`Authz: Org-admin` is deliberately not enforced in this module** — consistent with `FA-7` (client/command-layer permission checks are not the real authorization boundary; RLS is) and every other `§6.1`/`§11`-adjacent module's own established precedent of never checking caller role directly.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `api-specification.md §11.3` (`POST .../deactivate`, the empty-body request shape, `§8.1`/`§8.2`'s own Idempotency-Key mechanics); SRS `FR-009` (member deactivation preserves authorship, Must·P1, clean citation), `BR-023` (deactivated-member authorship retained, Must·P1 — the real citation, replacing `§11.3`'s own mis-cited `BR-024`, see mint note) |
| Goal | `deactivateMember` sets a membership's `status` to `DEACTIVATED` (never via the generic `PUT`, per `TASK-0095`'s own established exclusion) — idempotent via an `Idempotency-Key`, not `row_version`; a deactivated member retains authorship of all past events (naturally satisfied, since this module never touches anything but the one membership row) while being refused on any future write (enforced elsewhere, by whichever authorization layer checks `status` before a scoring write — out of this task's own scope, same boundary `FA-7` already draws). |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §11.3`, `§8.1`/`§8.2` (Idempotency-Key mechanics, read directly rather than assumed to mirror `row_version`); SRS `FR-009`/`BR-023`, read verbatim |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0095` (`memberships.ts`, whose `MembershipRow`/`MembershipStore` types this task reuses directly rather than redefining) |
| Inputs | `membershipId`, an `Idempotency-Key` string, the acting user's own `actorRef` |
| Expected behavior | `deactivateMember` first checks `IdempotencyStore.getPriorSuccess(key)` — if found, returns it unchanged, no further processing; else `404`s on a missing membership (not cached as a success, reprocessed fresh on retry); if already `DEACTIVATED`, returns the current row as a no-op success; else sets `status: "DEACTIVATED"`, bumps `rowVersion`, records the new result against the idempotency key, and returns it. |
| Acceptance criteria | `FR-009`'s own two acceptance lines (a deactivated member's write attempt is refused; past-event authorship still shows) — the first is enforced elsewhere (out of scope here, see Goal); the second is naturally satisfied, not actively implemented, since this module never touches past events at all. |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/deactivateMember.ts` (new module, reusing `memberships.ts`'s own `MembershipRow`/`MembershipStore`; a new `IdempotencyStore` interface, the first in this backlog) |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vitest run` genuinely executed; the identical-replay-on-same-key behavior, the fresh-reprocessing-after-a-failed-key behavior, and the already-deactivated no-op are all explicitly exercised |

#### TASK-0102 — API: `claimMatch` command (`POST /matches/{matchId}/claim`)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, the second `§11` match-lifecycle command task. **`invitations` (`§11.5`) was deliberately NOT picked next, despite being offered, after a real blocker was found during grounding, not silently worked around:** `data-specification.md` has **no `invitations` table anywhere in its own 28-table catalogue** — confirmed by searching the whole document for "invit" (zero matches) — and `domain-model.md`'s own membership Lifecycle (`Invited → Active ↔ Deactivated`) names an `Invited` state that `data-specification.md §3.3`'s own `memberships.status` enum (`ACTIVE`, `DEACTIVATED` only — two values) cannot even represent. There is genuinely no schema to persist a pending invitation (email, role, expiry, token) against. Per `ai-context-pack.md FA-9` ("it's fine to invent an endpoint/field/schema that 'seems missing'" is a false assumption — a real gap is a spec change, not a silent addition), this was not silently patched by inventing a table; `invitations` (`§11.5`) is parked pending a real schema addition, and `guest-match-claim` (`§11.7`) was picked instead — it operates entirely on the already-fully-specified `matches` resource (`TASK-0039`/`0044`), no new schema needed at all. **A fifth instance of the discovery-vs-SRS citation-namespace collision, the same same-number-different-meaning shape `TASK-0101` already found, now cross-category:** `api-specification.md §11.7`'s own Trace cites `FR-004, BR-022` — `FR-004` is clean (traces directly to discovery `FR-004`, Must·P1), but SRS `BR-022` is "Share links are read-only and revocable," wholly unrelated to guest-match-claim; the real entry is SRS `SEC-016` ("Guest-to-account boundary," Must·P1), which traces to **discovery** `BR-022` — a cross-category renumbering (a discovery-level `BR` became an SRS-level `SEC`, not even staying in the same ID family) on top of the usual same-number collision (SRS's own `BR-022` independently means something unrelated). `SEC-016` used instead. **A deliberate, reasoned DIFFERENCE from `TASK-0101`'s own "already-deactivated is a safe no-op" choice:** re-claiming an already-`CLAIMED` match (via a genuinely different Idempotency-Key) is rejected (`409 state/invalid-transition`), not treated as a no-op — unlike deactivation (whose end state doesn't depend on who deactivated first), claiming genuinely establishes ownership, so a second claim attempt by a different actor/organization would silently and wrongly succeed if treated as a harmless no-op; flagged as a considered departure from the immediately-preceding task's own precedent, not an inconsistency. **Reuses the Idempotency-Key pattern `TASK-0101` established**, per `§11`'s own intro ("every endpoint in this section... requires an Idempotency-Key") — this module's own `IdempotencyStore` is a second, independent instance of the same interface shape, not a shared one (idempotency is per-endpoint, `§8.2`).)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `api-specification.md §11.7` (`POST .../claim`, the `{ organizationId: uuid|null }` request shape, `§8.1`/`§8.2`'s Idempotency-Key mechanics); SRS `FR-004` (claim a guest match, Must·P1, clean citation), `SEC-016` (guest-to-account boundary, Must·P1 — the real citation, replacing `§11.7`'s own mis-cited `BR-022`, see mint note) |
| Goal | `claimMatch` transitions a match's `claimStatus` from `GUEST` to `CLAIMED`, optionally binding an `organizationId` (`null` = a personal, non-org claim) — idempotent via an `Idempotency-Key`, not `row_version`; a match already `CLAIMED` refuses a genuinely new claim attempt (`409`), since claiming establishes ownership and must not be silently re-grantable. `MatchId`, timeline, and provenance (`originDeviceId`/`createdAt`/`createdBy`) are untouched, per `FR-004`'s own acceptance line. |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §11.7`, `§8.1`/`§8.2` (Idempotency-Key mechanics); SRS `FR-004`/`SEC-016`, read verbatim; `data-specification.md §5.1`'s own `matches` field list (already fully captured in `TASK-0039`'s `MatchRow`) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0039`/`TASK-0044` (`matches.ts`, whose `MatchRow`/`MatchStore` types this task reuses directly); `TASK-0101` (the `IdempotencyStore` pattern this task's own module mirrors) |
| Inputs | `matchId`, `organizationId` (nullable), an `Idempotency-Key` string, the claiming user's own `actorRef` |
| Expected behavior | `claimMatch` first checks `IdempotencyStore.getPriorSuccess(key)` — if found, returns it unchanged; else `404`s on a missing match (not cached, reprocessed fresh on retry); `409`s (`invalidTransitionError`) if the match is already `CLAIMED` (a genuinely new attempt, not a key replay); else sets `claimStatus: "CLAIMED"`, `organizationId` to the payload's value, bumps `rowVersion`, leaves every other field (including `originDeviceId`/`createdAt`/`createdBy`) untouched, records the result against the key, and returns it. |
| Acceptance criteria | `FR-004`'s own two acceptance lines (`MatchId`/timeline/provenance unchanged on claim; a resolved local-team-to-registered-team link is recorded as an event, not an in-place edit — the second is out of this task's own scope, since it concerns `teams`/event-sourcing reconciliation this command doesn't touch) and `SEC-016`'s own line (an unclaimed guest match is invisible to any account's query — enforced at the RLS layer, not this module, per `FA-7`). |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/claimMatch.ts` (new module, reusing `matches.ts`'s own `MatchRow`/`MatchStore`; a second, independent `IdempotencyStore` instance mirroring `TASK-0101`'s own interface shape) |
| Verification procedure | §7 checks 7.1–7.2, 7.4–7.5; `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vitest run` genuinely executed; the identical-replay-on-same-key behavior, the fresh-reprocessing-after-a-failed-key behavior, the already-claimed rejection (contrasted explicitly against `TASK-0101`'s own no-op choice), and a null-`organizationId` personal claim are all explicitly exercised |

#### TASK-0103 — API: export-job tracker (`POST /matches/{matchId}/exports`, `GET /exports/{exportId}`, `§15`)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, picked per the user's own direction (`AskUserQuestion`) after all three remaining P1 `§11` commands (sign-off, account export/delete, auth) turned out to need real infrastructure this backend doesn't have yet — sign-off needs server-side reconciliation logic that only exists in `shared/`'s Kotlin core; account export/delete explicitly reuses this exact job mechanism (`§11.9`'s own text: "personal-data export reuses the same export-job mechanism as a match scorecard export"); auth endpoints are meant to be handled by Supabase Auth directly, not reimplemented here. Building this generic export-job tracker first unblocks `TASK-0101`-style account export/delete afterward, and is itself a well-specified, self-contained state machine. **A second genuine schema gap found, similar in shape to `invitations`' but smaller and safely buildable:** `data-specification.md` has **no `export_jobs`-style table anywhere** in its own 28-table catalogue — only a generic `EXPORT` category value inside `audit_log.category`'s enum, which records that an export *happened*, not a queryable job record with `status`/`downloadUrl`/`expiresAt`/`failureReason`. Unlike `invitations` (where even the field list was genuinely ambiguous), `api-specification.md §15.1`/`§15.2`'s own request/response contracts already give the **complete** field shape for this resource (`exportId`, `matchId`, `format`, `includeBranding`, `status`, `downloadUrl?`, `expiresAt?`, `failureReason?`) — this task's own `ExportJobRow` type is a direct transcription of that already-fully-specified API contract, not an invention; the gap is that `data-specification.md` itself hasn't caught up with `§15`'s own text yet, worth a future documentation fix, flagged rather than silently left implicit. **A sixth instance of the discovery-vs-SRS citation-namespace collision:** `§15`'s own header cites `NFR-030` for rate-limiting ("exports are comparatively expensive") — but SRS `NFR-030` is "Cricsheet schema conformance," unrelated; the real SRS entry for export rate-limiting is `SEC-010` ("Rate limiting and abuse protection"), which traces to **discovery** `NFR-030` — the same discovery-level-citation-collides-with-an-unrelated-SRS-entry shape found five times already. `SEC-010` is also `Should·P2` — genuinely not MVP scope per `FA-8` — so rate-limiting is correctly NOT implemented here, now with a citation-grounded reason rather than just "that's an infra-layer concern." **A real tension between the client-side and server-side export paths, flagged rather than conflated:** `FR-143`/`FR-144` (PDF/CSV export, Must·P1) both emphasize "offline, for locally held matches... produced on-device" — a client-side, offline-capable path — while `§15`'s own REST contract describes an asynchronous, server-side job writing to cloud Storage (`downloadUrl` is "a short-lived signed Storage URL"); this task builds the **server-side** job-tracking state machine only, grounded instead in `FR-150` (organization branding on exports, which needs server-side branding assets `includeBranding` implies) — `FR-143`/`FR-144`'s own on-device path is a separate, not-yet-built capability this task does not touch. **The actual file generation (PDF/CSV/Cricsheet rendering) is explicitly out of scope** — `§15.1`'s own text says exports are "always asynchronous... the contract is uniform regardless of size," meaning a worker process does the real rendering; this task builds the job-tracking state machine (`QUEUED→PROCESSING→READY|FAILED`) a worker would call into, not the rendering itself, the same "materialisation trigger, not the materialisation logic" scope boundary `§11.1`'s own sign-off note draws for reconciliation.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `api-specification.md §15.1/§15.2` (the complete request/response field shape, transcribed directly, not invented); SRS `FR-150` (organization branding on exports, Must·P1 — grounds the server-side path specifically); `FR-143`/`FR-144` (Must·P1, the separate client-side/offline export path this task does NOT build); `SEC-010` (rate limiting, `Should·P2`, correctly excluded — the real citation, replacing `§15`'s own mis-cited `NFR-030`) |
| Goal | `createExportJob` queues a new export job for a match (`QUEUED`), idempotent via `Idempotency-Key`; `getExportJob` returns the current job state; worker-side transition functions (`markExportProcessing`/`markExportReady`/`markExportFailed`) advance the job through its own state machine, validating every transition (no skipping `QUEUED`→`READY` directly, no mutating a terminal `READY`/`FAILED` job). The real PDF/CSV/Cricsheet rendering itself is out of scope — a future worker-process concern. |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §15.1/§15.2`, `§8.1`/`§8.2` (Idempotency-Key, reused from `TASK-0101`/`0102`'s own established pattern); SRS `FR-150`/`FR-143`/`FR-144`/`SEC-010`, read verbatim |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0039`/`0044` (`matches.ts`, the `matchId` FK target); `TASK-0101`/`0102` (the `IdempotencyStore` pattern this task's own module mirrors a third time) |
| Inputs | `matchId`, `format` (`PDF`/`CSV`/`CRICSHEET`), `includeBranding`, an `Idempotency-Key`, the requesting user's own `actorRef`; for the worker-side functions: `exportId`, and either a `downloadUrl`/`expiresAt` pair or a `failureReason` |
| Expected behavior | `createExportJob` schema-validates (`matchId`/`format` required, `format` one of the three), checks `IdempotencyStore.getPriorSuccess(key)` first, else inserts a new `QUEUED` row and returns `202`-shaped `{exportId, status: "QUEUED"}`; `getExportJob` `404`s on an unknown id; `markExportProcessing` only succeeds from `QUEUED`, `markExportReady`/`markExportFailed` only succeed from `PROCESSING` — any other starting state is `409 state/invalid-transition` (`invalidTransitionError`, reused from `TASK-0100`), since a job's own lifecycle is strictly linear and never revisited. |
| Acceptance criteria | No `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster case cites the export-job mechanism directly (checked) — grounded in `§15.1`/`§15.2`'s own contract text and `FR-150`'s own dependency chain (`FR-005`, `FR-143`). |
| Tests required | API tests (`§6`) |
| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/exportJobs.ts` (new module — the job state machine, a third independent `IdempotencyStore` instance) |

#### TASK-0104 — API: account data lifecycle (`POST /users/me/export`, `DELETE /users/me`, `§11.9`)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, the third `§11` match-lifecycle command task, unblocked by `TASK-0103`'s own export-job tracker per that task's own prediction. **A rich, four-citation correction, the seventh and eighth instances of the discovery-vs-SRS collision this session, both inside the same endpoint's own Trace line:** `§11.9`'s own Trace cites `FR-010/011` (both clean, Must·P1, verified earlier this session for `UX-27`), `BR-025`, `NFR-032`, `AUD-014`. Read directly: SRS `BR-025` is "Bowler over rules," wholly unrelated; SRS `NFR-032` is "Stable external identifiers," also unrelated. The real entries are `BR-023` ("Deactivated-member authorship retained," Must·P1 — the SAME SRS rule `TASK-0101` already used, since it traces to **both** discovery `BR-024` *and* `BR-025* (consolidated)* — each discovery number independently mis-cited at a different `§11` endpoint, both correctly resolving to this one SRS entry) and `NFR-038` ("Data-protection compliance... including data export and erasure," Must·P1, traces to discovery `NFR-032`). `AUD-014` ("Retention and anonymisation," Must·P1) was already correctly cited — its own Trace line (`discovery BR-025; NFR-032; SEC-015`) is the Rosetta stone that ties this whole correction together, independently confirming both substitutions. **A genuine on-topic citation found and added, the same "missing but on-topic" shape as `UX-27`'s `FR-155` and `UX-28`'s `BR-032`/`FR-008`/`FR-009`:** SRS `SEC-015` ("Export/erasure integrity," Must·P1) explicitly states "personal-data export and erasure shall be authenticated, authorised to the data subject..., and logged; erasure shall preserve Final match records with actor anonymisation (`BR-023`)" — a precise, direct match for this exact endpoint, cited nowhere in `§11.9`'s own Trace line at all. **A deliberate, flagged choice NOT to refactor `TASK-0103`'s already-committed `exportJobs.ts`:** `§11.9`'s own text says personal-data export "reuses the same export-job mechanism as a match scorecard export" — true code-level reuse would mean generalizing `ExportJobRow`'s `matchId`-specific field into a subject-agnostic shape; chosen instead to build a parallel, intentionally-duplicated state machine in this task's own new module, reusing the identical *pattern* (the same transition-guard shape, the same per-endpoint Idempotency-Key discipline) without touching a previously-merged, already-tested task's own contract uninvited. Flagged here as the honest tradeoff, not silently decided. **`DELETE /users/me` has no polling counterpart at all** — `§11.9`'s own text: completion is "communicated to the user by email..., not by polling this endpoint" — so unlike the export side, there is no `getAccountDeletion` function mapped to any client-facing endpoint; the store's own `get` is used only internally, for the idempotency/transition-guard checks. **A real idempotent-domain-state decision:** re-requesting deletion of an account already `PROCESSING` or `COMPLETED` (a genuinely new key) is a safe no-op, the same reasoning `TASK-0101`'s own deactivation no-op used — by the time deletion is `COMPLETED` the account is anonymised and effectively unable to re-authenticate to call this endpoint again anyway, making this mostly a defensive completeness case, not a load-bearing one. `users` itself is NOT a `§6.1` generic-CRUD resource — the original rule's own "10 remaining resources" count is exactly matched by the 9 built in `TASK-0093`-`0100` plus `matches`, confirming `users` was never in that list; this task models only the minimal `AccountDeletionStore` surface (`userId`/`status`/timestamps`) this specific command needs, not a full user-profile CRUD module, consistent with auth/profile fields being Supabase-managed (`§11.10`).)*

#### TASK-0105 — API: `signOffMatch` command, contract-only (`POST /matches/{matchId}/sign-off`)

*(Newly minted 2026-10-03, the fifth `§11` match-lifecycle command task, and the last tractable P1 one — auth endpoints (`§11.10`) are deliberately left unminted since Supabase Auth, not a custom backend command module, is this architecture's own intended provider for sign-up/sign-in/password/MFA; worth confirming with the user before ever minting that one, not assumed. **A ninth instance of the discovery-vs-SRS citation-namespace collision:** `§11.1`'s own Trace cites `FR-106, BR-005/007` — SRS's OWN `FR-106` is "Dispute lock and adjudication" (the same number `UX-28`'s own investigation already flagged as unrelated to sign-off), wholly unrelated here too; the real SRS entry is `FR-103` ("Head-scorer sign-off to Final," Must·P1), which traces to **discovery** `FR-106` directly. `BR-005`/`BR-007` check out cleanly — SRS's own `BR-005` ("Provisional until sign-off") and `BR-007` ("Reconciliation gate on finality") are both exactly on-topic. **A real on-topic citation found and added:** SRS `FR-102` ("Reconciliation gates sign-off," Must·P1) governs the override-reason-required-when-reconciliation-fails mechanism specifically, `FR-103`'s own sibling/dependency, absent from `§11.1`'s own Trace line entirely. **A deliberate, explicit contract-only scope boundary, the same shape `TASK-0033`'s own Android decision and `matches.ts`'s own `validateFrozenFields` approximation already established:** `§11.1`'s own note states this endpoint "re-runs `SVC-RECONCILER`/`SVC-RESULT-DERIVER` server-side" — neither service exists anywhere in `backend/` (both live only as pure Kotlin in `shared/`). This task takes the reconciliation check list and the server's own current event ordinal as **explicit caller-supplied inputs** rather than computing them. **Grounded directly in the already-built sibling `apps/web/src/screens/UX-22-match-summary/signOffForm.ts` (`TASK-0079`), mirrored field-for-field, not reinvented:** that module's own `ReconciliationCheck`/`Role`/`failingChecks`/`reconciliationPasses` shapes and its own `attemptSignOff` logic (role-check before reconciliation, `previousVersion + 1` incrementing) are duplicated here at the backend-command layer, the same "reuse the pattern, not the literal code across packages" approach `TASK-0104` already established for `exportJobs.ts`. **A real, corrected departure from this session's own "don't check roles" precedent, reasoned explicitly:** every other `§6.1`/`§11` module left role/authz checks to RLS per `FA-7` — but `§11.1`'s own error registry explicitly names `403 (not Head Scorer on this match)` as part of its OWN contract (unlike most other resources' Trace lines), and the sibling `signOffForm.ts` already implements exactly this check as real business logic, not merely advisory UI hiding; `signOffMatch` mirrors it, checked before reconciliation (`I-H2` before `I-H1`, per that module's own ordering). **Re-sign-off is supported, not conservatively rejected:** `data-specification.md §8.1`'s own `sign_offs` table explicitly supports "post-Final re-sign-off" (`version` 2, 3…, `supersedes_version`), and `signOffForm.ts`'s own `attemptSignOff` already increments unconditionally from a caller-supplied `previousVersion` — mirrored exactly here, rather than building a backend that's LESS capable than its already-shipped UI counterpart. A new `reconciliationBlockedError` (422, `reconciliation/blocked`) constructor is added to `errors.ts`, matching `signOffForm.ts`'s own `code: "reconciliation/blocked"` literal — the same "the registry had no constructor yet" gap `TASK-0100`'s `invalidTransitionError` already filled once.)*

|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `api-specification.md §11.1` (`POST .../sign-off`, the `{asOfEventOrdinal, overrideReason}` request shape, the `422`/`409`/`403` error registry); SRS `FR-103` (Head-scorer sign-off to Final, Must·P1 — replaces `§11.1`'s own mis-cited `FR-106`), `FR-102` (reconciliation gates sign-off, Must·P1 — a real on-topic citation missing entirely, added here), `BR-005`/`BR-007` (both correctly cited already); `data-specification.md §8.1` (`sign_offs` table — versioning/`supersedes_version`/immutability, read verbatim) |
| Goal | `signOffMatch` mirrors `signOffForm.ts`'s own `attemptSignOff` at the backend-command layer: `403`s if the caller isn't `HEAD_SCORER`; `409`s if `asOfEventOrdinal` is behind the server's current view; `422`s (`reconciliation/blocked`, naming the specific failing checks) if any check fails and no `overrideReason` is supplied; else persists a new, immutable `sign_offs` row at `previousVersion + 1` (`supersedesVersion` set only when `previousVersion > 0`). The real reconciliation computation (`SVC-RECONCILER`/`SVC-RESULT-DERIVER`) is explicitly out of scope, taken as caller-supplied input. |
