| Expected behavior | `signOffMatch` checks its idempotency store first; `403`s (`authForbidden`) if `actorRole !== "HEAD_SCORER"`; `409`s (`invalidTransitionError`) if `asOfEventOrdinal < currentServerEventOrdinal`; `422`s (`reconciliationBlockedError`, naming the failing checks) if any check fails and `overrideReason` is empty; else inserts `{id, matchId, version: previousVersion+1, signedBy, reconciliationState: "PASS"\|"OVERRIDE", overrideReason, signedAt, supersedesVersion: previousVersion>0 ? previousVersion : null}`, records it against the idempotency key, and returns it. |
| Acceptance criteria | `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster-H's own three cases, already proven at the UI layer by `signOffForm.ts` and mirrored here: `N-H1` (passing reconciliation signs off, `version` increments by exactly 1), `I-H1` (an open `FAIL` with no override is rejected, naming the specific failing checks), `I-H2` (a non-Head-Scorer attempt is rejected, checked before reconciliation). |

#### TASK-0119 — Schema RCR: `invitations` table + `memberships.invited_at`/`accepted_at` columns

*(Picked per the user's own explicit direction via `AskUserQuestion`, once all 13 Android counterparts (`TASK-0106`-`0118`) closed out and this session returned to `§6.3`'s deferred list: the only item there that's a bounded structural fix rather than a priority-scope override only the user can authorize. New `data-specification.md §3.4` `invitations` table (PK `id`, UQ `token`, status `PENDING`/`ACCEPTED`/`REVOKED` — no `EXPIRED` value, since expiry is a function of `expires_at < now()` at read/accept time, not a stored state). No change to `memberships.status`'s existing enum/CHECK — the schema fix is purely additive.)*

|---|---|

| Goal | `inviteMember` validates and inserts a `PENDING` `invitations` row with a server-generated `token`; `acceptInvitation` looks the row up by token, `410`s if expired, `409`s if already `ACCEPTED`/`REVOKED` (not a silent no-op — a reused token being replayed is a genuinely different concern than `deactivateMember`'s own idempotent-domain-state no-op, since a token is single-use by design), else inserts the real `memberships` row (`status: ACTIVE`, `roles` copied from the invitation, `invited_at`/`accepted_at` populated) and marks the invitation `ACCEPTED` |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §11.5`, `§5.1`/`§5.2` (error shape/registry); `data-specification.md §3.4`/`§3.3` (`TASK-0119`'s own new/extended tables, read verbatim); `domain-model.md`'s `CMD-INVITE-MEMBER`/`CMD-ACCEPT-INVITE` |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0119` (the schema this task is built on); `TASK-0095` (`memberships.ts`, whose `MembershipRow`/`MembershipStore` this task reuses via cross-module import rather than redefining — the same cross-module-reuse shape `TASK-0114`'s `deactivateMember` already established against the same module) |
| Inputs | **Send:** `organizationId`, `email`, `roles`, `expiresAt`, `invitedBy` (actor ref), a new `invitationId`/`token`, `nowIso`. **Accept:** `token`, `acceptingUserId` (the authenticated caller's real `users.id` — `§11.5`'s own "authenticated as the invited email" Authz line is an auth-layer check this module has no session plumbing to perform, taken as an explicit input instead of derived from `invitation.email`), `nowIso`, a new `membershipId`. |
| Expected behavior | **Send:** `400`s (`schemaValidationError`) on a missing `email`/empty `roles`/invalid role (reusing `memberships.ts`'s own `VALID_ROLES` list, `TASK-0108`, rather than redefining it); else inserts `{id, organizationId, email, roles, token, status: "PENDING", expiresAt, invitedAt: nowIso, invitedBy, acceptedAt: null}`. **Accept:** `404`s (`notFoundError`) on an unknown token; `410`s (`invitationExpiredError`) if `expiresAt < nowIso`; `409`s (`invalidTransitionError`) if `status !== "PENDING"`; else inserts a new `memberships` row (`status: "ACTIVE"`, `roles` from the invitation, `invitedAt`/`acceptedAt` populated), marks the invitation `{status: "ACCEPTED", acceptedAt: nowIso}`, and returns the new `memberships` row, per `§11.5`'s own Response text verbatim. Neither endpoint uses the `Idempotency-Key` mechanism — `send` is a `POST` that genuinely creates a new resource each call (no stated idempotency contract in `§11.5`'s own text, unlike every other `§11` command); `accept`'s own natural idempotency key *is* the token itself (a second accept attempt on an already-`ACCEPTED` token is correctly a `409`, not a silent replay, since re-running accept would otherwise create a second `memberships` row for the same invitation — flagged explicitly as a deliberate departure from the `IdempotencyStore` pattern every other `§11` command task has used, not an oversight). |

*(The first task pulled from `§6.3`'s deferred V1/V2/Future list, not the originally-tractable P1 set — a deliberate, explicit priority-scope override, not silently treated as if always MVP. Picked via `AskUserQuestion` among `§6.3`'s remaining items (the user asked for the model's own recommendation, then said to proceed); the cleanest of the three remaining options since `FR-039`/`BR-031`/`BR-044` are each a single, already-fully-specified `§11.6` contract and — unlike `invitations` — the schema already has everything needed (`data-specification.md §4.2`'s own `status`/`merged_into_player_id` columns on `players`, built early this session and already structurally excluded from `players.ts`'s own create/update payloads in `TASK-0096`, reserved for exactly this task). **A real, significant architectural boundary found and flagged, not silently resolved:** `FR-039`'s own text says a merge "re-points all historical appearances to the survivor" — taken completely literally, this would mean rewriting `player_id` references inside `match_events` (the hash-chained, append-only event store, `MINV-01…03`) and its direct read-model descendants (`deliveries`/`overs`/`wickets`/`batter_card_lines`/`bowler_card_lines`), breaking the hash-chain integrity invariant established as early as `TASK-0007`/`0014`. **This task does not do that** — it implements only the literal, minimal contract `§11.6`'s own Request/Response text specifies (flip the losing `players` row; return the surviving row unchanged); "re-pointing" elsewhere is left as a read-time resolution through `merged_into_player_id`, never a bulk rewrite of immutable history — the acceptance criterion's own wording ("the losing ID **resolves to** the surviving ID") supports this reading as much as a literal-rewrite one, and only this reading is compatible with the event store's own immutability. **The `422` conflict check (`§11.6`'s own "overlapping matches on the same date needing manual review" note) needs appearance-date data `players.ts`'s own `PlayerStore` cannot provide** (appearances live in `matches.home_xi`/`away_xi` and the scoring read model) — modeled as an explicit `PlayerAppearanceLookup` port, the same "take the missing service as a caller-supplied input" shape `signOffMatch.ts` (`TASK-0105`) already used for `SVC-RECONCILER`. **`§11.6`'s own "with the merge logged" text (`AUD-002`) is not implemented** — `data-specification.md §10`'s own `audit_log.category` enum already lists `PLAYER_MERGE` (and marks `reason` required-by-application-logic for exactly this category, matching this endpoint's own request field one-for-one), but no backend module anywhere in this codebase has ever written a literal `audit_log` row — the same "a natural extension point, not wired up by this task" deferral `authorize.ts`/`exportJobs.ts` each already state. `Authz: Org-admin or platform-admin` left to RLS per `FA-7`, the default every `§6.1`/`§11` module except `signOffMatch` already uses.)*

|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `FR-039` (`Should·P2` in the SRS, `V2·Should` in `product-roadmap.md` — a deliberate priority-scope override, flagged, not treated as MVP); `BR-031` (`Must·P2`, player-merge authority — authz left to RLS, not re-checked here, same `FA-7` default); `BR-044`/`MINV-18`; `AUD-002` (merge logging — not implemented, see mint note); `api-specification.md §11.6`; `data-specification.md §4.2` (`players.status`/`merged_into_player_id`, already modeled); `domain-model.md`'s `CMD-MERGE-PLAYERS` |
| Goal | `mergePlayers` validates the request, rejects a self-merge or a merge involving an already-`MERGED` player, rejects (`422`, naming the specific dates) if the two players have overlapping appearance dates per `§11.6`'s own conflict note, else marks the losing `players` row `{status: "MERGED", merged_into_player_id: survivingPlayerId}` and returns the untouched surviving row |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §11.6` (full Request/Response/Errors text, read verbatim); `data-specification.md §4.2` (`players`' own `status`/`merged_into_player_id`/`CK`); SRS `FR-039`/`BR-031`, read verbatim; `product-roadmap.md`'s own `V2·Should` bucket tag, confirmed directly (not assumed) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0096` (`players.ts`, whose `PlayerRow`/`PlayerStore` this task reuses via cross-module import rather than redefining — the same cross-module-reuse shape `TASK-0114`'s `deactivateMember` already established) |
| Inputs | `survivingPlayerId` (path parameter), `losingPlayerId`, `reason`, a `PlayerAppearanceLookup` port (caller-supplied, see mint note), `actorRef`, `nowIso` |
| Expected behavior | `400`s (`schemaValidationError`) on a missing `losingPlayerId`/`reason`; `422`s (`businessRuleValidationError`) on a self-merge attempt or an overlapping-appearance-date conflict (naming the specific conflicting dates); `404`s (`notFoundError`) if either player id doesn't resolve; `409`s (`invalidTransitionError`) if either player is already `MERGED`; else updates the losing row (`status: "MERGED"`, `mergedIntoPlayerId: survivingPlayerId`, `rowVersion + 1`) and returns the surviving row, per `§11.6`'s own Response text verbatim — the surviving row itself is never mutated by this operation. |

| Files/components expected to change | `backend/src/commands/mergePlayers.ts` (new module) |

#### TASK-0122 — Schema RCR: `disputes` table + `matches.state` `DISPUTED` value

*(The second task pulled from `§6.3`'s deferred list, picked via `AskUserQuestion` on the model's own recommendation among the three remaining options — chosen over the `UX-27` interface-language item (needs i18n infrastructure built from scratch) and the other `UX-28` sub-items (vague, infra-shaped, tangled with the unbuilt Competitions epic) since it is a single FR with an already-fully-specified API contract. Resolves a gap `api-specification.md §11.4`'s own text assumed already resolved: "Lock request... Response 200: `matches.state` transition reflected" names a transition the `state` enum (`data-specification.md §5.1`) had no value for. **A real domain-model tension found and flagged, not silently resolved:** `domain-model.md`'s own `ENT-DISPUTE` lives entirely under the unbuilt `CTX-COMPETITION`/`AGG-COMPETITION` context (seasons/fixtures/standings, `V2` everywhere else it has come up this session) — this RCR follows `§11.4`'s own match-scoped contract instead, since a match can be disputed with no competition/fixture involved at all; the competition-scoped half (`BR-015`'s standings-exclusion) remains genuinely unbuilt. New `data-specification.md §8.6` `disputes` table models `locked_from_state` explicitly — the match's own pre-lock `state`, restored verbatim on adjudication, since a lock could originate from `IN_PROGRESS`/`INNINGS_BREAK`/`PAUSED`/`COMPLETE` and must return to exactly that value, not a hardcoded one. `status` is collapsed to a two-value `OPEN`/`ADJUDICATED` enum (vs. `domain-model.md`'s own three-value `OPEN`/`UNDER_REVIEW`/`ADJUDICATED`), since `§11.4`'s own two-endpoint contract has no separate "mark under review" step — a deliberate simplification tracking the actual endpoint pair, not the richer competition-scoped lifecycle. Two new migration files mirror both changes as real SQL (never executed in this environment, same limitation as every migration since `TASK-0001`): `20260923000033_create_disputes.sql` (table + a single org-member-visible `SELECT` RLS policy) and `20260923000034_matches_add_disputed_state.sql` (drop-and-recreate the existing `CHECK` constraint, since Postgres has no `ALTER CHECK`).)*

|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `FR-106` (corrected from `§11.4`'s own mis-cited `FR-112`); `BR-016`; `AUD-012`; `api-specification.md §11.4`; `data-specification.md §8.6`/`§5.1` (`TASK-0122`'s own new/extended tables) |
| Goal | `lockMatchForDispute` validates and transitions a match to `DISPUTED`, recording its pre-lock state; `adjudicateDispute` records the ruling and any authorised correction-event ids, then restores the match to exactly that pre-lock state |
| Context needed | `api-specification.md §11.4` (full Lock/Adjudicate contract, read verbatim); `data-specification.md §8.6`/`§5.1` (`TASK-0122`'s own schema, read verbatim); SRS `FR-106`, read verbatim (correcting `§11.4`'s own `FR-112` mis-citation) |
| Dependencies | `TASK-0122` (the schema this task is built on); `TASK-0039`/`0044` (`matches.ts`, whose `MatchRow`/`MatchStore`/widened `MatchState` this task reuses via cross-module import rather than redefining) |

#### TASK-0055 — Web: `UX-10 Live Scoring` screen (derived-display layer)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-25, opening the Live Scoring cluster (`UX-10`-`19`) now that Match Setup (`UX-04`-`09`) is complete. A third instance of the discovery/SRS numbering-namespace collision confirmed before trusting the citation: `UX-10`'s Trace cites `FR-063/065`, which is **SRS `FR-063`** ("Persistent live-state panel," `Must/P1`, `software-requirements-specification.md:771`) **doubled with discovery's own `FR-065`** ("Display persistent live state...," an independent, matching description) for the *same* requirement — **not** SRS's own separate `FR-065` ("Wicket-keeper change mid-innings," unrelated, `Could/P2`) and **not** discovery's own separate `FR-063` ("end-of-over checkpoint," a different, `UX-16`-adjacent requirement). **Deliberately narrow scope, not the whole hub:** `UX-10`'s own Validation line says "Not applicable directly (display + navigation)" — there is no per-state action-availability matrix written anywhere in this corpus (checked, not assumed), so inventing one here would be fabricating unspecified product design. This task instead builds exactly what IS concretely specified: the derived-display computations `cricket-rules-reference.md RUN-013`/`TGT-005` define precisely, plus a flagged, explicitly-interpretive state-derivation helper. The ball-recording interactions themselves (`UX-11`-`13`), the bowler-change/over-completion prompts (`UX-14`-`16`), and DLS par (out of scope backlog-wide per `§6.3`/`SPK-01`) are separate, later tasks.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `ux-specification.md UX-10`; SRS `FR-063`/discovery `FR-065` ("Persistent live-state panel," see note above); `cricket-rules-reference.md RUN-013` (run rate/required run rate/projected score formulas); `TGT-005` (chase display requirements: runs required, balls remaining, required run rate, DLS par/target); `NFR-012` (100% offline) |
| Goal | Given the live innings state (runs, wickets, legal balls bowled, overs allotted, optional target), compute the exact display values `UX-10` and `TGT-005` require: overs in `O.B` notation, current run rate, runs required, balls remaining, and required run rate — correctly, including the subtle case `RUN-013` specifies precisely (rate math uses **true decimal overs** — legal balls ÷ balls-per-over — not the `O.B` display notation misread as a decimal). |
| Context needed | `ux-specification.md UX-10` (Purpose/Inputs/Actions/Validation/States/Error handling/Accessibility); `cricket-rules-reference.md RUN-013`/`TGT-005`; SRS `FR-063`'s own Acceptance bullet ("Given a live chase, when any delivery is recorded, then runs required and balls remaining update immediately and correctly") |

| Inputs | Runs scored, legal balls bowled, overs allotted, balls per over, optional target (null in the first innings) |
| Expected behavior | `formatOvers`/`oversAsDecimal` correctly distinguish `O.B` display notation from the true decimal used in rate math (`RUN-013`); `computeRunRate` returns the correct rate and a defined value (not a divide-by-zero crash) at zero balls bowled; `computeRunsRequired`/`computeBallsRemaining`/`computeRequiredRunRate` match `RUN-013`/`TGT-005` exactly, with `null` (not `0` or `Infinity`) for the well-defined "not applicable" cases (no target set; zero balls remaining). |
| Acceptance criteria | No `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster case cites `UX-10` directly (checked, not assumed; cluster D's rich `CE-*` cases are about the delivery-processing engine, already covered by `shared/`'s own pipeline tests since `TASK-0017`+, not this display layer) — grounded directly in `cricket-rules-reference.md RUN-013`/`TGT-005` (exact formulas) plus SRS `FR-063`'s own Acceptance bullet. |

#### TASK-0063 — Web: `UX-14 Strike Change` screen (manual-override layer)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-25, continuing the Live Scoring cluster. **A genuine finding, not assumed:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/pipeline/StrikeResolver.kt` (`TASK-0025`-era) implements `live-scoring.md §14.2`'s automatic strike-derivation formula, but its own "HONEST SCOPE NOTE" explicitly states `§14.5` (manual override) is "**Not implemented here**" anywhere in the pipeline — this task is the first real coverage of the override mechanism itself, not a UI layer over already-built logic like `TASK-0059`/`0061` were. `UX-14`'s Trace (`DR-10`, `MBR-09`, `STRK-*`) has no bare `FR-`/`BR-` numbers, so the usual discovery/SRS namespace-collision check doesn't apply here — `MBR-09` (`domain-model.md:864`) and `DR-10` (`software-requirements-specification.md:1785`) both confirmed directly, no ambiguity found.)*

|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `ux-specification.md UX-14`; `live-scoring.md §14.5` (Manual override — the authoritative rule text); `domain-model.md`'s `CMD-OVERRIDE-STRIKER`/`EVT-STRIKER-OVERRIDDEN` (`newStrikerId`, `reason`, `STRK-011`, `MBR-09`); `DR-10` (Strike Rotation & End Changes) |
| Goal | A scorer can swap the current striker/non-striker with a mandatory reason, producing an `EVT-STRIKER-OVERRIDDEN`-shaped submission; the override applies to exactly one delivery — the automatic `§14.2` derivation resumes from the next delivery onward, never "sticky." |
| Context needed | `ux-specification.md UX-14` (Purpose/Inputs/Actions/Validation/States/Error handling/Accessibility); `live-scoring.md §14.5`'s exact rule text |
| Dependencies | `shared/`'s existing `StrikeResolver.kt` (`TASK-0025`) for the auto-derived display half — reused, not reinvented; the override half is new |
| Inputs | Current striker/non-striker (display) |
| Expected behavior | `toggleSwap`/`cancelSwap`/`confirmOverride` implement the Auto → Pending-override → Overridden state flow (`UX-14`'s own States text); `confirmOverride` rejects a blank reason (`§14.5`: "requires a non-empty reason"); the produced override swaps the current pair (`newStrikerId` = the current non-striker) — matching `domain-model.md`'s `CMD-OVERRIDE-STRIKER` shape (`newStrikerId` singular, not a free pick from the wider XI). **Flagged, not resolved:** `UX-14`'s own Error-handling text ("An override with no not-out batters available to swap to is not reachable") implies a richer not-out-batter candidate pool than a strict two-person swap can ever have (the swap partner is always exactly 1 person, never an empty pool under that model) — this task implements the strict-swap model since it's what `§14.5`'s rule text and `CMD-OVERRIDE-STRIKER`'s own shape most directly support, with this tension noted as an open reconciliation question, not silently resolved either way. |
| Acceptance criteria | No `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster case cites `UX-14` directly (checked) — grounded in `§14.5`'s own rule text plus `UX-14`'s own Validation/Error-handling/States prose. |

Trace: `BR-028`.") — `UX-10` through `UX-14` all had none.)*

*(Newly minted 2026-09-25, continuing the Live Scoring cluster — the screen `UX-16`'s "Jump to Score Correction" action opens onto. **A major grounding discovery:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/pipeline/InningsCorrector.kt` (`TASK-0029`-era) already implements `live-scoring.md §19.1`'s correction/refold/cascade-summary logic in full (`correctDelivery()`, `CascadeSummary`, `InningsEndTimingChange`) — but its own "HONEST SCOPE NOTE" explicitly says it does **not** emit `EVT-DELIVERY-CORRECTED` or enforce `§19.3`'s post-Final elevated-role precondition, calling both "orchestration/authz concerns... which those concerns wrap around." **This task is exactly that wrapper** — not a re-mirror of `correctDelivery()`'s own cascade math (which stays server/engine-side, consumed here as a caller-supplied result), but the two genuinely missing pieces: the mandatory-reason gate (`AUD-005`) and the post-Final elevated-role gate (`BR-006`/`§19.3`). **Clean citations throughout:** `BR-004`/`BR-006` match exactly across both the discovery and SRS namespaces this time (no collision, `software-requirements-specification.md:1849/1865` both cite "discovery BR-004"/"discovery BR-006" directly) — unlike the higher-numbered `BR-027…036` collisions found in `TASK-0059`/`0061`/`0065`; `FR-097…103/108` are SRS-level and correctly this screen's own territory.)*

| Goal | A scorer can review a pre-computed cascade summary (from `shared/`'s existing `correctDelivery()`) and, given a mandatory reason and — if the match is Final — an elevated role, commit the correction; without either, Save stays blocked with a specific, stated reason, never a flat refusal. |

#### TASK-0071 — Web: `UX-18 Undo` screen (redo-window state-machine layer)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-25, continuing the Live Scoring cluster. **Reuse confirmed, sixth time this cluster:** `shared/src/commonMain/.../core/pipeline/InningsFolder.kt`'s own `undoLastDelivery(initialState, deliveries, config)` already exists (`fun undoLastDelivery(...) = foldInnings(initialState, deliveries.dropLast(1), config)`) — a one-line, already-tested, deterministic reversal. This task does not reimplement it. **A genuine citation incompleteness found, distinct from `TASK-0061`'s missing-FR case:** `UX-18`'s Trace `FR-059/061/062` mixes SRS-level `FR-059` ("Undo the last action") with discovery-level `FR-061`/`FR-062` ("Undo"/"Redo an undone action") — but **SRS has its own `FR-060`, "Redo an undone action,"** which traces directly to discovery `FR-062` (`software-requirements-specification.md:744`), and it is never cited at all, even though its discovery-level counterpart is. Recorded here so `FR-060` isn't mistaken for uncited/unimportant — it's this screen's own real Redo requirement, just missing from the Trace line.)*

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Requirement IDs | `ux-specification.md UX-18`; SRS `FR-059` ("Undo the last action") = discovery `FR-061`; SRS `FR-060` ("Redo an undone action," **not in `UX-18`'s own Trace, flagged above**) = discovery `FR-062` |
| Goal | Undo reverses the single most recent action, calling the already-existing `undoLastDelivery`; Redo restores it if — and only if — nothing new has been entered since; the redo window closes the instant a new entry is recorded. |
| Context needed | `ux-specification.md UX-18` (Purpose/Inputs/Actions/Validation/States/Error handling/Accessibility); `shared/`'s existing `undoLastDelivery` (the reversal this screen's Undo action calls, not reimplements) |
| Dependencies | `shared/`'s existing `InningsFolder.undoLastDelivery` (`TASK-0029`) — reused, not reinvented |
| Inputs | Whether a most-recent action exists; whether a guardrail modal is currently open |
| Expected behavior | `performUndo` moves `AVAILABLE → JUST_UNDONE`, capturing the undone action generically (typed, not shape-specific — this screen doesn't know a delivery's own structure); `performRedo` only succeeds from `JUST_UNDONE`, per `UX-18`'s own Validation text ("Redo is available only immediately after an Undo, before any new entry"); `onNewEntryRecorded` closes the redo window unconditionally. **Flagged, not resolved:** `UX-18`'s own Error-handling text describes Undo routing to `UX-17` "on the rare event [it] cannot fully reverse a complex multi-part action" — but the underlying `undoLastDelivery` fold is a complete, deterministic reversal by construction (re-folding from genesis), so this escape hatch describes a case the pure fold function structurally cannot produce; the escape hatch is implemented as a caller-supplied capability flag (`canFullyReverse`), since only the orchestration layer (which knows whether "the last action" spans more than one delivery/event) can determine when it applies. |
| Acceptance criteria | No `acceptance-criteria.md` cluster case cites `UX-18` directly (checked) — grounded in `UX-18`'s own Validation/Error-handling/States prose plus the reused `undoLastDelivery`. |

*(Newly minted 2026-09-28, closing out the Connectivity cluster (`UX-23`-`25`). **A major scoping finding, the cleanest V1/V2 split found this session:** `UX-25`'s own Purpose text explicitly tags its two concerns differently — "dual-scorer divergences **(V2)**" vs. "device-fence conflicts **(P1, one scorer/multiple devices)**." Per `FA-8` (a requirement not explicitly Must/P1 is not MVP scope) and the backlog's own `§6.3` deferral rule, this task is scoped to the P1 fence-conflict half only; the V2 divergence half is deferred, not built. **Striking corollary: every single citation in `UX-25`'s own Trace line (`FR-107/108/109/110/111`, `BR-008`, `MINV-14`, `SYNC-011/012/013/014` — ten numbers) belongs exclusively to the deferred V2 divergence half — zero citation anywhere covers the P1 fence-conflict half this task actually builds.** That logic's real authority is `offline-first-specification.md §11.1`, which is what `shared/`'s already-existing `core/sync/WriterFenceConflict.kt` (`TASK-0037`) itself cites. **Real reuse confirmed, a seventh `core/sync/` hit:** `WriterFenceConflict.kt`'s own `FenceConflictResolution` enum (`TAKE_OVER`, `DISCARD_LOCALLY` — "no third, automatic option exists... a fence conflict is a workflow question a human must answer") already matches `UX-25`'s own Actions text exactly ("Take over scoring on this device" / "cancel and keep the original device active").)*

#### TASK-0091 — Web: `UX-28 Administration` screen (org-admin role/invite layer + platform-admin impersonation/feature-flag/reference-data layer, MVP subset)

*(Newly minted 2026-09-28, the final screen in the `§6.2` UX-screen decomposition. **A genuinely new methodological finding, worth recording precisely because it complicates every earlier P1/MVP scope call this session:** `product-roadmap.md §How to read this roadmap` defines **MVP as "a strict subset of P1"** and **Version 1 as "the remainder of P1"** — i.e. `P1` phase splits into two roadmap buckets, `MVP` and `V1`, and a `Should·P1` SRS entry can land in either. This session's own established working boundary (per the memory record: "`UX-04`-`25`, P1 scope, ALL complete") has consistently targeted the full **P1 phase** (`MVP` ∪ `V1`), not the narrower `MVP` bucket alone — consistent with the sheer breadth of what's already built (sync/conflict-resolution/DLS-adjacent machinery far beyond a literal "smallest slice that scores one match" MVP). **This means `FA-8`'s own shorthand ("Should/Could items are later releases") gates on P2+/`V2`+/Should-or-Could-*within-a-later-bucket*, not on the `MVP`/`V1` split itself** — a `V1·Must` or `V1·Should` roadmap tag is still in this session's own established scope, same as any other `P1`-phase item already built. **Flagged, not silently corrected:** `TASK-0089`'s own deferral of SRS `FR-157` (interface language) cited its roadmap tag as "V1·Should" as if that meant out-of-scope — under this more precise reading it likely should NOT have been excluded on bucket grounds alone (its real blocker, not stated clearly enough at the time, is that no string-externalization/i18n infrastructure exists anywhere in this codebase to build even a minimal placeholder against — an infrastructure gap in the same family as `TASK-0033`'s Android-toolchain scope decision, not a pure MVP-boundary exclusion). Not reopened here without being asked; flagged for the user's awareness and to correct this task's own reasoning going forward. **Applying the corrected reading to `UX-28` itself:** SRS `FR-159` ("Organization administration console") is `Must·P2` **with its own explicit carve-out, "(Should·P1 for the invite/roles subset)"** — so member/role management is in scope, the rest (player registry, competition/template config, branding) is not, per the SRS's own parenthetical, independent of resolving the bucket question. Likewise `FR-160` ("Platform administration and operations") is `Must·P2` **with its own carve-out, "(Should·P1 for staged rollout + reference-data updates)"** — feature-flag/staged-rollout and reference-data-publish are in scope; tenant-management/monitoring are not, confirmed by `product-roadmap.md`'s own separate lines ("Staged rollout, feature flags, release rollback" tagged `V1·Should`; "Org-level operational tooling: monitoring, sync-backlog dashboards" tagged `V2·Should`). `FR-161`/`SEC-011` (impersonation) are `Must·P1` outright — in scope, no carve-out needed. **`FR-039` (player merge) is genuinely out of scope under any reading** — `Should·P2` at the SRS level, and `product-roadmap.md` tags its own discovery-level features `V2·Should` (`FR-039`/`FR-040`/`BR-044`) — the same triple-confirmed exclusion shape as `TASK-0085`'s clean V2 finding. **`FR-106`/`BR-016` (dispute lock/adjudication) are also genuinely out of scope** — `Should·P2` — and correctly absent from `UX-28`'s own Trace line already (no citation gap here, unlike the cases below). **A real citation gap found:** `BR-032` ("Reference-data versioning," `Must·P1`) matches `UX-28`'s own "publish reference data" Action precisely and is not cited in `UX-28`'s own Trace line at all — added to this task's own citation list, the same shape as `TASK-0089`'s `FR-155` finding. **A real citation-namespace note:** `UX-28`'s own `BR-044` citation is a discovery-only number — SRS consolidated it as `BR-031` (`Must·P2`, dependent on the now-deferred `FR-039`) — both numbers noted, neither drives this task's built scope. **Real, concrete acceptance-criteria grounding found for once:** `acceptance-criteria.md SEC-06`/`SEC-07` directly ground the impersonation-consent-flow build (unexpired-consent gate; dual-identity audit entries) — unlike most screens this session, this one has real cluster-case citations, not just the screen's own prose. **Two more real citation gaps, on top of `BR-032`:** `FR-008` ("Email invitations," Should·P1, clean discovery match) and `FR-009` ("Member deactivation preserves authorship," Must·P1, clean discovery match) match `UX-28`'s own "Invite a member" and "Deactivate a member" Actions precisely and are absent from `UX-28`'s own Trace line entirely — both added to this task's own citation list. **`shared/`-reuse check: confirmed absent** (searched `core/{model,pipeline,sync}` for role/RBAC/permission/impersonation/feature-flag logic — none exists), expected, same shape as `UX-26`/`27`'s findings.)*

### 6.3 V1/V2/Future scope (E-25, E-26)

Not decomposed at all yet, deliberately — per `ai-context-pack.md FA-8` (a requirement not explicitly Must/P1 is not MVP scope) and `FA-14` (an open item like `SPK-01` is not resolved by picking a default in a task). When `SPK-01` (DLS licensing) or `SPK-04` (cross-platform parity spike) resolve, or a release gate in `product-roadmap.md` opens V1 scope, apply the same method as §4: pick the epic's richest-specified sub-area first (for DLS, that's `cricket-rules-reference.md`'s DLS rule set and the `RainMethod` strategy interface `ADR-10` already names), and decompose it the same way — pipeline/schema/API/UI, small, dependency-ordered.

**`UX-25`'s dual-scorer-divergence half (V2, `TASK-0085`/`0086`'s own field tables flag this explicitly) — the propose/confirm RESOLUTION-COMMAND piece is now RESOLVED, 2026-10-04.** Was deferred: side-by-side per-over.ball comparison, propose/confirm reconciliation, and the divergence-blocks-sign-off gate (`FR-107…111`, `BR-008`, `MINV-14`, `SYNC-011…014` — all ten of `UX-25`'s own Trace citations belong here, not to the P1 fence-conflict half that task actually built). Genuinely `V2`·Should at every level (SRS priority, roadmap bucket, and MoSCoW) — pulled into scope anyway per the user's own explicit direction (asked for the model's own recommendation among the remaining `§6.3` items, then said to proceed with it), a deliberate, flagged priority-scope override, not treated as if it had always been MVP. Built as `TASK-0121` (`backend/src/commands/mergePlayers.ts`) — see that task's own entry below and `§9`'s changelog for the full resolution, including a real architectural boundary found: the merge command only flips the losing `players` row (`status=MERGED`/`merged_into_player_id`), it does NOT rewrite `player_id` references inside the hash-chained `match_events` event store or its read-model descendants, since that would break `MINV-01…03`'s own immutability invariant — "re-pointing historical appearances" is implemented as a read-time resolution through `merged_into_player_id`, not a bulk historical rewrite. (4) **RESOLVED (partially), 2026-10-04** — platform tenant management/monitoring, the P2 remainder of `FR-160`. Re-reading `FR-160`'s own text found it names five distinct remaining capabilities, not one lump: tenant management, feature flags, health/sync-backlog monitoring, reference-data updates (already built, `TASK-0100`), status/maintenance banners. **Feature flags' own storage layer, the one piece with an existing UI shell already built but no backend behind it** (`UX-28`'s own `AdministrationScreen.tsx` already renders a `FEATURE_FLAGS` section, `TASK-0091`, but takes `featureFlags`/`onToggleFeatureFlag` as caller-supplied props with no backend persistence wired) — built as `TASK-0132`, see that task's own entry below. **Tenant management (suspend/activate) — RESOLVED, 2026-10-04.** The cross-document gap `TASK-0132`'s own mint note flagged (`domain-model.md`'s `ENT-ORGANIZATION` names an `ACTIVE`/`SUSPENDED`/`DELETED` status `organizations` had no column for) turned out to be `data-specification.md §13`'s own explicitly-flagged open item `DSQ-2` ("whether `organizations` needs a soft-delete/decommission path... revisit if an org-offboarding flow becomes a requirement") — not a silent gap, a documented open question. Asked the user explicitly whether to resolve it or leave it deferred; the user said resolve it. Built as `TASK-0133` (schema RCR resolving `DSQ-2` + `backend/src/commands/organizationLifecycle.ts`: `suspendOrganization`/`reactivateOrganization`/`deleteOrganization`) — see that task's own entry below. **Status/maintenance banners — RESOLVED, 2026-10-04, the most speculative build of the entire session.** Asked the user directly for a recommendation among the three remaining `§6.3` items (status banners / configure competitions / health-monitoring); recommended banners as the smallest, most contained option despite having zero textual anchor anywhere in this corpus beyond the single phrase "status/maintenance banners" in `FR-160`'s own Description line — no acceptance criterion, no UX text, no domain-model entity, unlike every other storage-only slice this round. The user confirmed building it anyway. Built as `TASK-0138` (schema RCR: new `data-specification.md §10.3` `status_banners` table + `backend/src/commands/statusBanners.ts`) — see that task's own entry below. **Still genuinely deferred, unchanged:** health/sync-backlog monitoring (real ops/observability tooling, outside this app's own domain model) and "configure competitions" (needs the entirely unbuilt Competitions epic).

Per the user's own explicit direction (picked "Invitations schema fix" over the other `§6.3` items, which all still require a priority-scope override only the user can authorize), `TASK-0119` resolved the schema gap with an RCR (new `data-specification.md §3.4` `invitations` table; two new nullable columns on `memberships`) and `TASK-0120` built the command module itself. ## 7. Traceability and coverage statement

Every task above cites at least one real ID from `ai-context-pack.md §14`'s legend — no task in this document uses a requirement ID that does not exist in the corpus (self-check per `ai-development-harness.md §7.2`, applied to this document's own authoring). §3's epic map accounts for every top-level area of the Master Specification, whether decomposed to task grain yet or explicitly marked pending. This document does **not** claim 100% requirement-to-task coverage at the individual `FR-*`/`DR-*`/`BR-*` level — it claims, consistent with the corpus's established asymmetric-mapping precedent, that every requirement lands in a *named, findable place* (an epic, and either a task or a stated generation rule) — which is the coverage bar `api-specification.md §19`, `testing-strategy.md §19`, and `acceptance-criteria.md §11` each already set for themselves.

| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0017` (`DeliveryInput` model & V1–V11 validation): Merged, with three honest scope decisions.** Implemented `model/{DeliveryInput,DismissalMode,Legality,RunEvent,ValidationFailure,WicketDetail}.kt` and `pipeline/DeliveryValidator.kt`, plus `commonTest/.../DeliveryValidatorTest.kt` covering all 6 mandatory `§21.9` cases (`C50…C55`) and extra coverage for `V1/V2/V7/V8` (not exercised by `§21.9`). **Scoped to `§5` (V1–V11) only, not `§4`'s guardrails** — the backlog's own wording called V1–V11 "the eleven guardrail checks," but `live-scoring.md` uses "guardrail" specifically for `§4`; kept the narrower, acceptance-criteria-matching scope. `V1` implemented as a documented no-op (structurally guaranteed by the `Legality` enum); `V10` deliberately not implemented (depends on `§4`'s guardrail stage, out of scope) rather than written as a check that could never verify anything. `V7`/`V8` are conservative — `V7` only covers `CAUGHT`; `V8` doesn't handle the legitimate-resumption exception (`§9.6`). `DismissalMode` correctly uses 10 values, matching `AVF-SE-01`, cited directly rather than the "11" `§9.2`'s prose claims. **Self-review caught and fixed one real issue** (no compiler exists to catch it): `WicketDetail`'s field order had a defaulted parameter before a required one — safe only because every call site used named arguments; reordered to remove the risk. `TASK-0018` (legality classification) is now unblocked. |

| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0019` (`RunEvent` model & core run formulas): Merged, clean.** Implemented `pipeline/RunAggregator.kt` + test — `§7.2`'s five aggregate formulas (`total`, `batterRuns`, extras-by-category, `bowlerRunsCharged`, `ranRuns`), verified against 13 of `§21.2–21.4`'s 23 cases, hand-checked against 5 by formula before any code was written given zero compiler safety net. Confirmed `§7.6`'s "boundary subsumes" rule needs no special-case logic — it falls out of which `RunEvent`s the caller constructs, not extra logic in the aggregator. `shortRuns` (`§8`) deduction correctly excluded — `TASK-0020`'s separately-listed scope, not this one's; `aggregateRuns` returns the pre-short-run-adjustment raw values. `TASK-0020` (extras decomposition & short runs) is now unblocked. |

Implemented per the actual spec text (authoritative over the backlog's paraphrase), with a test reproducing that exact worked example. `ExtrasDecomposer.kt` covers the genuinely distinct "which `RunEvent`s to construct" half of this task's goal, cross-checked against the same `C13/C14/C16/C19/C21/C22` cases already hand-verified in `TASK-0019`. `TASK-0021` (dismissal-mode-validity matrix) is now unblocked. |

What `§21.7` (this task's actual acceptance criteria) needed instead was bowler credit (`§9.4`) — implemented as `creditsBowler()`/`BOWLER_CREDITED_MODES` in `model/DismissalMode.kt`, verified against all 14 `§21.7` cases. **Also closed a flagged gap from `TASK-0010`:** `wickets.credits_bowler` had no `CHECK` constraint because the mapping wasn't confirmed at the time — added as addendum migration `20260923000030_wickets_credits_bowler_check.sql`, now that `§9.4` gives it exactly. | 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0024` (Team score & over-completion state rules, `§12–§13`): Merged, with one real ambiguity resolved and one gap flagged.** Implemented `model/{OverState,InningsScoreState}.kt`, `config/PlayingConditionsProfile.kt` (the typed CFG-REG shape `TASK-0016`'s `ReferenceDataPort` doc comment had flagged as "not yet modeled" — scoped to exactly the one key this task needs, `format.balls_per_over`, not the full registry), and `pipeline/InningsOverStateUpdater.kt` (`updateInningsScoreState`, `updateOverState`). **Ambiguity resolved, not glossed over:** `§12`'s `totalRuns` row, read literally, double-counts a batting-side `PENALTY` (already inside `RunAggregate.total` per `§7.2`'s own side-blind formula) while separately saying to add "any PENALTY awarded to the batting side" — resolved by treating the operative instruction as `§12`'s own third sentence: subtract a fielding-side-awarded `PENALTY` back out of `total` before crediting it to `totalRuns`; `extras.penalty` stays unconditional per `§12`'s literal wording (records that a penalty happened, not who it benefited). **Flagged gap, not silently dropped:** a `PENALTY` awarded to the fielding side is correctly excluded from the batting side's `totalRuns` here, but `§7.7` also requires crediting it to the *other* team's own running total — no structure spanning both sides' innings exists yet to receive that credit. Verified by hand against every `§22` example reporting team-score/over-state figures, including the flagship `EX-03`/`EX-04` over-boundary parity pair (`legalBallCount` reaching `ballsPerOver` completes the over identically regardless of how the runs on the final ball arose) plus one self-constructed case (no worked example covers a fielding-side penalty) derived directly from `§7.7`'s text. `previousOverBowlerId`/`BR-027` and next-over bowler selection deliberately left to the orchestration layer — `§4` guardrails are out of scope for every task so far, same reasoning `TASK-0017`'s `V10` was skipped. `TASK-0025` (strike resolution) is now unblocked. |
| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0025` (Strike resolution, `netRotates` XOR formula, `§14.2`): Merged, scoped exactly to the formula per the task's own title.** Implemented `pipeline/StrikeResolver.kt` (`resolvesStrikeRotation`, `applyStrikeRotation`/`StrikePositions`). `isOverFinalLegalBall` is deliberately taken as a precomputed `Boolean` rather than re-derived — it's exactly `TASK-0024`'s `OverUpdateResult.isOverComplete` for the same delivery, since §14.2's own definition of "the over's final ball" is identical to how that flag is already computed there; no duplicate logic. `effectiveRanRuns` must come from a `RunAggregate` built off the §8-adjusted `RunEvent` list (`TASK-0020`'s `applyShortRuns()`), the same "adjusted list in" contract already established by `TASK-0022`. **All four XOR truth-table combinations verified against real `§22` examples, not synthetic cases:** `EX-01` (even/not-final → no rotation), `EX-02` (odd/not-final → rotates), `EX-03` (even/final → rotates purely from the end-of-over swap, `ranRuns=0` confirmed since `BOUNDARY` never contributes), `EX-04` (odd/final → the run-swap and end-swap cancel, the case implementers most often get backward), `EX-05` (a wide can never be "the over's final ball" at all, confirmed independent of its own `ranRuns`). **Honest scope decision:** §14.3 (wicket interaction), §14.5 (manual override), §14.6 (mankad, which skips this formula entirely) are orchestration rules about when/how to call this formula and combine it with `TASK-0043`'s `resolveEndPositions()` — not additional computation the formula itself needs, and not implemented here; flagged in the file's own doc comment rather than silently expanded into. `TASK-0026` (innings/match-end evaluation) is now unblocked. |
| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0026` (Innings-end and match-end evaluation, `§15`): Merged, with one deliberate scope narrowing flagged.** Implemented `model/InningsEndReason.kt` (`ALL_OUT`/`OVERS_COMPLETE`/`TARGET_REACHED`) and `pipeline/InningsEndEvaluator.kt`'s `evaluateInningsEnd()`, in §15's own fixed priority order (all-out checked first, then overs-complete, then target-reached — first condition met wins, verified by two dedicated priority-order tests where more than one condition is simultaneously true). Takes plain primitives, not a full `InningsState` aggregate, matching this pipeline's established style (`LegalityClassifier` takes a bare `Legality`, not a whole `DeliveryInput`). **Scope narrowing, flagged not silently done:** §2's full `InningsState` shape (`inningsId`, `battingTeamId`, `bowlingTeamId`, `target`, `freeHitPending`, `strikerBatterId`, `nonStrikerBatterId`, `currentBowlerId`, `previousOverBowlerId`) still does not exist as a single assembled type anywhere in this backlog — `InningsScoreState` (`TASK-0024`) only ever carried §12's score-aggregate subset; assembling the real aggregate is orchestration-layer work for a later task, not this pure evaluator's job. §15.4 (declaration/forfeiture) correctly excluded — the spec's own text calls it captain-initiated, not delivery-triggered. Verified against `§22.8`'s `EX-11` (a `RETIRED_NOT_OUT` batter reduces the effective all-out threshold by exactly 1, `MINV-10`) plus the task's own stated Expected Behavior (target reached mid-over ends the innings immediately — tested with `legalBallsBowled` deliberately short of the over's completion). `TASK-0027` (event schemas) is now unblocked. |
| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0027` (`EVT-DELIVERY-RECORDED` and related event schemas, `§16`): Merged — a real error in this task's own backlog entry found and corrected, not silently followed.** Implemented `model/MatchEvent.kt` (`DeliveryRecorded`, `NonStrikerRunOut`, `StrikerOverridden`, `PlayingConditionsFrozen`) and `pipeline/EventGenerator.kt` (`deliveryRecordedEvents`, `nonStrikerRunOutEvent`). `DeliveryRecorded` wraps the existing `DeliveryInput` type directly rather than re-declaring its 11 fields — §16.2's own field table lists exactly `DeliveryInput`'s shape as "copied... verbatim." **Error found:** this task's own Expected Behavior field claimed a non-striker run-out "additionally emits `EVT-NON-STRIKER-RUN-OUT` alongside the base delivery event, per §16's stated co-emission rule" — no such rule exists; `§16.3`'s actual text says `EVT-NON-STRIKER-RUN-OUT` is emitted **instead of** `EVT-DELIVERY-RECORDED`, only for the pre-delivery mankad case (§9.6) — a replacement, never an addition; a normal in-play `RUN_OUT` of the non-striker carries its `WicketDetail` inside `EVT-DELIVERY-RECORDED`'s payload like any other wicket and gets no separate event at all. Implemented per §16's actual text; the backlog field itself corrected in place above, with the original wording struck through and dated rather than silently replaced. **Scope split, flagged:** the generic event envelope (`eventId`, `actorRef`, `provenance`, `prevHash`/`hash`, etc.) is deliberately not modeled here — it's `TASK-0028`'s explicit `§17` scope and already exists as `match_events` columns (`TASK-0006`); this task models only each event type's domain-specific payload. **Open edge case flagged, not resolved either way:** §16.5 ties `EVT-PLAYING-CONDITIONS-FROZEN` specifically to the very first `EVT-DELIVERY-RECORDED`, not the match's first event of any kind — if a mankad genuinely happens before any ball is ever bowled, the literal spec text leaves the conditions-frozen event un-emitted until the actual first legal delivery, possibly several deliveries later; noted in `EventGenerator.kt`'s own doc comment. `TASK-0028` (audit record generation) is now unblocked. |
| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0028` (Audit record generation per delivery, `§17`): Merged, with a real Acceptance-Criteria mismatch flagged.** Implemented `model/AuditedEvent.kt` (`EventProvenance`, `AuditedEvent`) and `pipeline/AuditEventGenerator.kt`'s `auditEvent()` — the first pipeline function in this backlog to compose `TASK-0016`'s `ClockPort`/`IdPort`, per §1.1's determinism boundary (`eventId` from `IdPort.newId()`, `recordedAt` from `ClockPort.nowEpochMillis()`, never a direct UUID/system-clock call). `AuditedEvent` **is** the "`AuditRecord`" this task's own Files/components field named — §17's own opening line states "every event in §16 is its own audit record," so this is the generic attribution envelope wrapping a `MatchEvent` (`TASK-0027`) that that task's own doc comment explicitly deferred here, not a separate type alongside it. §17's five numbered requirements verified: (1) `actorRef` non-nullable by construction; (2) `provenance` a required constructor argument, never backfilled; (3) `prevHash`/`hash` modeled as required fields but **not computed** — flagged as a real, standing gap, since no canonical `MatchEvent` serialization format exists anywhere in this backlog yet (`EventLogPort`'s own `TASK-0016` doc comment already deferred this); (4) `overrideReason` verified to survive unseparated inside the wrapped event (test asserts it round-trips through `DeliveryInput`/`DeliveryRecorded` unchanged); (5) the same `auditEvent()` function verified to handle a `WIDE` delivery identically to a `LEGAL` one — no per-legality branching. **Mismatch flagged, not silently ignored:** this task's own Acceptance Criteria cites `security-specification.md §11`'s `SR-I01…I04` — read in full, all four are database-grant/scheduled-job/API-authz requirements (append-only grants, admin-audit-log completeness, a scheduled hash-chain verification job, role-gated audit-trail read access), none of which are shared/ Kotlin domain-model concerns at all. `SR-I01` is already satisfied by `TASK-0007`'s grants; `SR-I02`/`I03`/`I04` remain unimplemented anywhere in this backlog — a real gap for whichever future backend/infra task actually owns a scheduled verification job and admin-plane audit wiring, not something this type can satisfy by itself. `TASK-0029` (undo/void-and-refold) is now unblocked. |
| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0029` (Undo, void-and-refold, `§18`): Merged — the first orchestration task in this backlog, wiring `TASK-0017`-`0026`'s pure functions into an actual Steps 5-11 fold engine, with real gaps found and fixed during self-review, plus three flagged, deliberately unresolved edge cases.** Implemented `model/InningsFoldState.kt` (`InningsFoldState`, `FoldConfig` — the full per-innings projection aggregate every prior E-09..E-13 task deferred assembling), widened `config/PlayingConditionsProfile.kt` with `freeHitOnNoBall` (needed to fold `§13.4`), added `MatchEvent.DeliveryVoided` to `TASK-0027`'s sealed class (`§18.1` step 2 names this event but, unlike `§16.2-16.5`, never gave it its own field table — modeled minimally per the one field §18.1's text actually specifies), and `pipeline/InningsFolder.kt` (`updateFreeHitPending`, `applyDelivery`, `foldInnings`, `undoLastDelivery`). `undoLastDelivery` is literally `foldInnings` over `deliveries.dropLast(1)` — Undo needs no delivery-type-specific logic at all, per §18.1's own central claim, verified structurally rather than merely asserted. **Bug caught and fixed during self-review, before any test was run against it:** the first draft never reset `OverState` when a new over began mid-fold — `updateOverState()` deliberately never does this itself (the caller's job, per `OverState`'s own doc comment), and the first draft never supplied that caller-side reset, which would have made every over after the first accumulate `legalBallCount` past `ballsPerOver` forever. Fixed by checking `legalBallCount == ballsPerOver` at the start of each `applyDelivery` call and constructing a fresh `OverState` (new `overNumber`, `bowlerId` from the delivery already being processed) before folding that delivery in. **Verified against real `§22` examples across every delivery type the task's own Expected Behavior names:** `EX-01` (dot ball, plus its own Undo claim reproduced exactly), `EX-03`/`EX-04` (the flagship over-boundary parity pair), `EX-08` (caught — undo removes the incoming batter's card line entirely and restores the outgoing batter to `NOT_OUT`), and `EX-12`'s own compound claim (over/strike/score all revert together automatically across an over boundary, with no bespoke "un-complete the over" code) — reproduced structurally via a dedicated test. A general batch-vs-incremental-folding agreement property is also checked across a longer mixed sequence, directly exercising this task's own stated Verification procedure ("refolded state is byte-identical to the pre-delivery state"). **Three edge cases flagged, deliberately not resolved:** (1) the pre-delivery mankad (`§9.6`) is not foldable by `applyDelivery` at all — it has no `DeliveryInput` to fold (`§16.3`'s own text: "minus legality... none apply"), and would need a parallel, differently-shaped fold step not built here; (2) `§14.3` step 3's claim that the end-of-over swap applies "on top of" a wicket-resolved striker/non-striker pairing is implemented per its best-supported literal reading, but no `§22` worked example combines a wicket with the over's final ball to verify it against; (3) Step 1 (validation, `§5`) is deliberately never re-run during a fold — `§18.1`'s own step list names "Steps 5-11" only, and re-validating already-accepted active events is out of this function's scope. `TASK-0030` (correction/supersede-and-refold) is now unblocked. |
| 0.1.0 | 2026-09-23 | **`TASK-0030` (Correction, supersede-and-refold, incl. innings-end-timing-changed edge case, `§19`): Merged.** Implemented `pipeline/InningsCorrector.kt` (`foldInningsToEnd`, `foldTrace`, `firstStrikeContinuityBreak`, `correctDelivery`), reusing `TASK-0029`'s `applyDelivery` entirely — §19.1 step 4 is explicit that recomputation is "identical mechanism to §18.1 step 3." 'committed' and 'queued for sync' can never diverge" — `OutboxState.pending()` computes directly from `committedEventIdsInOrder` minus `acknowledgedEventIds`, structurally ruling out the divergence `§6.1` warns against, rather than relying on two separate mutations staying in sync by convention. `executeOfflineDeliveryCommand` composes `TASK-0029`'s `applyDelivery` (local projection, pure) + `TASK-0033`'s `DurableEventLogWriter.commit` (durable persistence) + `recordCommit` (outbox) as one function — `§4.2`'s "offline commands execute immediately, fully, against local state... only the transmission... is queued" made structurally checkable: the function's own signature has no network port reachable from it at all, so `FA-15` ("the network is never on this path") holds by construction, not merely by test assertion. **Scope boundary, deliberate:** `§6.2`'s full "what is queued" list (every `§16` event type, publish/notify intents with their own idempotency key) and `§6.5`'s backpressure/chunking policy are not modeled — this task's own Requirement IDs are `§4` only, and `TASK-0035` (Push protocol) is the task that actually consumes and extends this outbox against a real transport, per its own Inputs field ("the local outbox from `TASK-0034`"). | 0.1.0 | 2026-10-03 | **`TASK-0100` (API: `reference_data` CRUD — `PUT` publish-new-version-only, `GET` list, `GET` one, `GET` latest; no update, no `DELETE`): Merged — the ninth and final `§6.1`-generated generic-CRUD task, closing out `§6.1`'s entire resource list.** Built the real shared table (`data-specification.md §5.4`) faithfully across all three `kind`s (`CONDITIONS_PROFILE`/`DLS_TABLE`/`APP_CONFIG`) rather than narrowing to the one `api-specification.md §10.2`'s own "Condition templates" row names, the same reasoning `teams.ts` already applied. **Two genuine spec tensions found and flagged, not silently resolved:** (1) `§10.2`'s own path is `/organizations/{orgId}/condition-templates` — a nested, org-scoped URL — but `§5.4`'s own field table has **no `organization_id` column at all**; `reference_data` is genuinely global (PK `(kind, version)`), consistent with its own "devices cache a pinned snapshot" pull-only sync model. No `organizationId` field was invented to match the URL — likely a stale vestige from an earlier design never reconciled with the final schema. (2) `§10.2` says publishing is "platform/org-admin only"; `§5.4`'s own field-level note restricts `published_by` to platform-admin specifically — the narrower of the two; this module accepts any `publishedBy` without a role check at all, per `FA-7` (RLS is the real boundary, not this layer). **The strictest immutability model in this backlog:** `§5.4`'s own text — "a published `(kind, version)` row is never updated in place" — combined with `§10.2`'s explicit "`409`... not a silent overwrite" leaves no idempotency exception even for an identical resubmission, unlike every other resource's reliance on `§8.3`'s general PUT-idempotency; `publishReferenceData` requires the caller's `version` to be exactly `currentHighest + 1`, rejecting an already-existing version (`409`, a genuinely new `invalidTransitionError` constructor added to `errors.ts` for `§5.2`'s own `state/invalid-transition` registry entry, which had no constructor yet — distinct from `staleVersionError`'s `concurrency/stale-version`) and a version that skips ahead (`422`). **`DELETE` is not merely unbuilt for lack of policy — it's explicitly, permanently ruled out:** `§5.4`'s own Soft-deletion line reads "none — every historical version must remain resolvable," the strongest exclusion found this session. **A third citation-correctness finding, this time inside `api-specification.md` itself:** `§10.2`'s own row cites `FR-016`/`FR-028` for this resource — read directly, `FR-016` is "Create an ad-hoc match" and `FR-028` is "Minimum-overs-for-result configuration," neither related to publishing reference data at all; not inherited as validated citations. Clean citation instead: SRS `BR-032` (reference-data versioning, Must·P1, already used once this session for `UX-28`'s own reference-data-publish Action). A `getLatestReferenceData` function was added, directly grounded in `§5.4`'s own `IX: kind` note ("to fetch the latest quickly"), not invented. **Genuinely executed:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` — **188/188 passed** (175 pre-existing from `TASK-0001…0099` + 13 new: 7 publish + 2 get-one + 2 list + 2 latest). No Android counterpart. **`§6.1`'s own generic-CRUD resource list (`api-specification.md §10.2`'s full table) is now completely exhausted** — every resource from `matches` (`TASK-0039`/`0044`) through `reference_data` is built. Only `§11`'s match-lifecycle commands remain undecomposed within `§6.1`'s own rule, alongside `§6.3`'s already-tracked V1/V2/Future scope. |
| 0.1.0 | 2026-10-03 | **`TASK-0101` (API: `deactivateMember` command, `POST .../memberships/{id}/deactivate`): Merged — the first `§11` match-lifecycle command task, picked per the user's own direction once `§6.1`'s generic-CRUD list closed out.** **A fourth instance of the discovery-vs-SRS citation-namespace collision, the richest variant yet — a true same-number-different-meaning collision layered on top of the renumbering itself:** `api-specification.md §11.3`'s own Trace line cites `FR-009, BR-024` — but SRS `BR-024` is "Toss determines initial innings order," wholly unrelated to member deactivation; the real SRS entry is `BR-023` ("Deactivated-member authorship retained," Must·P1), which traces to **discovery** `BR-024`+`BR-025` *(consolidated)* — `§11.3` cited the discovery-level number directly, AND that same number independently means something unrelated at the SRS level, the worst-case combination of the two collision shapes found separately before now (`UX-28`'s clean renumbering; `UX-22`'s same-number-different-meaning). `BR-023` used instead. **The first task to need `§8`'s Idempotency-Key mechanism, not `row_version`:** `§11.3`'s own Request shape is literally `{}` — no `row_version` at all, confirming `§8.3`'s own framing that command/RPC endpoints use Idempotency-Key (`§8.1`/`§8.2`) instead. Implemented per `§8.2`'s own precise behavior: a key that already produced a **successful** result returns that identical prior result, never recomputed; a key that previously **failed** is reprocessed fresh next time, never cached. An honest scope note: `§8.1`'s own `[DEFAULT]` 24-hour retention window isn't modeled — the in-memory store retains indefinitely, the same simplification every other in-memory store here makes. **A real idempotent-domain-state decision, reasoned rather than guessed:** re-deactivating an already-`DEACTIVATED` membership (via a different key) is a safe no-op returning the current row, not an error — no spec text says a second deactivation should fail. `Authz: Org-admin` deliberately not enforced, per `FA-7`. Clean `FR-009` citation (Must·P1). `backend/src/commands/deactivateMember.ts` reuses `memberships.ts`'s own `MembershipRow`/`MembershipStore` directly rather than redefining them. **Genuinely executed:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` — **194/194 passed** (188 pre-existing from `TASK-0001…0100` + 6 new). No Android counterpart. |

Per `FA-9`, not silently patched by inventing a table; tracked in `§6.3` as a genuine schema gap, not a priority deferral, and `guest-match-claim` picked instead since it needs no new schema at all. **A fifth instance of the discovery-vs-SRS citation-namespace collision, now cross-category:** `§11.7`'s own Trace cites `FR-004, BR-022` — `FR-004` is clean, but SRS `BR-022` is "Share links are read-only and revocable," wholly unrelated; the real entry is SRS `SEC-016` ("Guest-to-account boundary," Must·P1), which traces to **discovery** `BR-022` — a discovery-level `BR` became an SRS-level `SEC`, not even staying in the same ID family, on top of the usual same-number collision (SRS's own `BR-022` independently means something unrelated). `SEC-016` used instead. **A deliberate, reasoned DIFFERENCE from `TASK-0101`'s own "already-deactivated is a safe no-op" choice:** re-claiming an already-`CLAIMED` match (via a genuinely different Idempotency-Key) is rejected (`409`), not treated as a no-op — claiming establishes ownership, so a second claim attempt by a different actor would silently and wrongly succeed if harmless-no-op'd, unlike deactivation whose end state doesn't depend on who acted first; flagged as a considered departure, not an inconsistency. Reuses `matches.ts`'s own `MatchRow`/`MatchStore` directly and mirrors `TASK-0101`'s own `IdempotencyStore` interface shape as a second, independent instance (idempotency is per-endpoint). Provenance (`originDeviceId`/`createdAt`/`createdBy`) verified untouched by the claim, per `FR-004`'s own acceptance line. **Genuinely executed:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx vitest run` — **200/200 passed** (194 pre-existing from `TASK-0001…0101` + 6 new). No Android counterpart. |

The real PDF/CSV/Cricsheet rendering itself is out of scope — a worker-process concern; this module builds only the state machine (`QUEUED→PROCESSING→READY|FAILED`) a worker would call into, enforcing every transition strictly (no skipping `PROCESSING`, no mutating a terminal `READY`/`FAILED` job). No Android counterpart. |

mirrored here, checked before reconciliation (`I-H2` before `I-H1`). **Re-sign-off is supported, not conservatively rejected** — `data-specification.md §8.1`'s own `sign_offs` table explicitly supports "post-Final re-sign-off" (`version` 2, 3…, `supersedes_version`), and `signOffForm.ts` already increments unconditionally from a caller-supplied `previousVersion`; mirrored exactly, avoiding a backend less capable than its already-shipped UI counterpart. A new `reconciliationBlockedError` (422, `reconciliation/blocked`) constructor was added to `errors.ts`, matching `signOffForm.ts`'s own error-code literal — the same "the registry had no constructor yet" gap `TASK-0100`'s `invalidTransitionError` already filled once. | 0.1.0 | 2026-10-03 | **`TASK-0106` (Android: `organizations` API contract, `com.kencric.scoring.api.organizations`): Merged — the first of 13 Android counterparts for the `§6.1`/`§11` backend command modules, picked up per the user's own explicit direction once the backend command-layer backlog closed out.** Establishes a new third top-level Kotlin namespace, `com.kencric.scoring.api.*` (alongside `core.*` and `ui.*`, confirmed by listing the existing top-level packages before picking a name, not guessed) — `com.kencric.scoring.api.errors.ApiErrors.kt` mirrors `backend/src/authz/errors.ts` as it stands after `TASK-0105` in full (`ApiProblem` + all seven error constructors), built once and reused by every subsequent task in this cluster, the same role `errors.ts` plays on the TS side. `Organizations.kt` is a field-for-field port of `organizations.ts` (`TASK-0093`) — no new citation research, every decision was already resolved when that module was first built. **A genuine, real design finding, not just mechanical translation:** `UpdateOrganizationPayload.rowVersion` had to be made nullable (`Int?`, not `Int`) to honestly port the TS test's own "missing rowVersion" case — the TS payload type declares `rowVersion: number` as required but that specific test reaches the missing-field branch via `{} as UpdateOrganizationPayload`, an unsafe-cast escape hatch Kotlin has no equivalent of; without nullability, that case would be structurally unrepresentable. **A real, flagged smart-cast-safety correction, caught before committing, not after a compiler (which doesn't exist here) could catch it:** the test file's first draft used `assertTrue(result is SomeSealedType)` followed by direct field access on `result` — `assertTrue` carries no contract, so the Kotlin compiler cannot smart-cast afterward, the exact "unverifiable smart-cast risk" this backlog has flagged since `TASK-0041`; this is the FIRST Android test in this codebase to need field access after a sealed-class narrowing at all (every prior `ui.screens.*` port only ever checked `is` as a bare boolean), so there was no existing precedent to follow — fixed throughout using `check(result is X)` (a contract-bearing stdlib function), the correct, compiler-safe idiom, established here for every subsequent task in this cluster to reuse. Hand-traced the full module end-to-end against its own TS source line-for-line (the standing discipline for every `shared/` Kotlin file, no compiler existing in this environment) — `isNullOrEmpty()`'s own contract correctly smart-casts `query.after`/`query.nameSearch` after each null-check, `minOf`/`sortedBy`/`take` all verified against Kotlin stdlib signatures. 17 parity tests reproduce all 17 of `organizations.test.ts`'s own cases input-for-input — the first draft of this port omitted its own `updateOrganization_missing_row_version_is_schema_failure_400` case entirely; caught during a line-by-line recount against the TS source and added, rather than shipping an incomplete parity suite. No real Kotlin/Gradle toolchain exists in this environment — verification is hand-tracing only, the same standing limitation as every `shared/` file this entire backlog. |

`FR-160`'s is "(Should·P1 for staged rollout + reference-data updates)" — so this task's own built scope follows those carve-outs directly, independent of resolving the bucket question at all. **`FR-039` (player merge) is genuinely out of scope under any reading** — `Should·P2` at the SRS level AND `product-roadmap.md`'s own explicit `V2·Should` tag, the cleanest triple-confirmed exclusion this session; `FR-106`/`BR-016` (dispute lock/adjudication) likewise `Should·P2`, and — unlike every other deferred item this session — already correctly absent from `UX-28`'s own Trace line (no citation-gap fix needed there). **Three real citation gaps found and added:** `FR-008` (email invitations, Should·P1), `FR-009` (member deactivation, Must·P1), and `BR-032` (reference-data versioning, Must·P1) all match `UX-28`'s own Actions text precisely and were absent from its own Trace line. **A real citation-namespace note:** `UX-28`'s own `BR-044` is a discovery-only number; SRS consolidated it as `BR-031` (`Must·P2`, dependent on the now-deferred `FR-039`) — both noted, neither drives built scope. **For once, real acceptance-criteria grounding exists:** `acceptance-criteria.md SEC-06`/`SEC-07` directly ground the impersonation-consent-flow build. **Confirmed absence of reusable `shared/` logic** (role/RBAC/impersonation/feature-flag), expected, same shape as `UX-26`/`27`'s findings. **Built:** `visibleAdminSections` (platform sections hidden entirely for org-admins, not disabled), `addRole`/`removeRole` (the additive model — adding never removes another role), `confirmAdminDestructiveAction` (explicit confirm gate on deactivate/revoke), `requestImpersonation` (refuses on missing OR expired consent, per `SEC-06`), `impersonationAuditActor` (dual-identity audit naming, per `SEC-07`), `nextReferenceDataVersion` (always a new version id, never overwrite, per `BR-032`), `isAdminActionAvailable` (read-heavy views work offline;
